  - **Recommend A** — a record page recurs in every business app (a lead, a client, an estimate), has its own parts and a state, which is what a term is; B misnames a section and C leaves the one piece the row reports (the collapsed state) unowned. The ninth answer is a spec: `anatomy.spec.js` already fails a part with no CSS and a class the vocabulary does not name.

- **FJS-D1164 — How does a widget's type scale stop following the host page's root font size?** `rem` always reads the DOCUMENT root, a shadow root does not change that, and the kit spells size in rem throughout (`tokens.css` `--text-*` and `--space-*`, ~244 literals over 65 files in `css/src` and `ui/components`) with no `:host` rule setting a size. The red test: mount a widget under a host with `html { font-size: 10px }` and assert `getComputedStyle(shadowRoot.querySelector('.btn')).fontSize` equals what a 16px host gives (today 10.36px at 360 wide against ~14–16px). Mesa component styles reach the root through `adoptedStyleSheets` at runtime, not through `widgetCssPlugin`, so a build-time rewrite of the bundle's CSS alone leaves them on rem.
  - **A** — Rewrite `Nrem` to `calc(N * var(--rem, 1rem))` in every stylesheet that enters a widget root, with `:host { --rem: 16px }` set by `mountInto`. One owner for the widget boundary, but it must cover BOTH the `widgetCssPlugin` output and Mesa's runtime-registered sheets (mesa is the leaf and knows no widget), and `@media`/`@container` conditions in rem stay on the host root. The widget stops honoring a reader's raised base font.
  - **B** — `mountInto` measures the host's root size and sets `:host { zoom: calc(16 / <host px>) }`. No CSS rewrite and the kit stays untouched, but every px (borders, ring widths, a px `max-width`) scales by the same factor, and `zoom` shifts popover and measured-position arithmetic.
  - **C** — Make the kit stop spelling size in rem for widgets: `@frontierjs/css` states its sizes through a `--rem` token that `:root` sets to `1rem` and `:host` sets to `16px`, so every literal becomes `calc(N * var(--rem))`. The same rewrite as A moved into the source, so Mesa component sheets are covered by being written that way, at the price of touching 65 files and a test that no new bare `rem` appears.
  - **D** — Document it as a host's responsibility (the 62.5% root is theirs) and ship nothing. The marketing-site hosts a widget lives on are the common case, so the row's measurement stays true.
  - **Recommend C** — one spelling in the source is the only option that covers Mesa's runtime sheets without teaching mesa about widgets, and a guard test keeps it from regressing.

- ~~**FJS-D1165 — What is a second, independent filtered list of one resource: a list that states its own `where` owning its rows, a documented `composed: true`, or a new option?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-17 (`FJS-D1165`): A — a list that states `where` (or its own `query`) keeps its own rows, fed by its `load()` answer and kept live from pushes through `matchesQuery`; a list stating none stays the resource's store. `composed` keeps its meaning (includes). The doc line is one sentence: *a list over a filter is its own.*.** Found fixing `FJS-1444`, reproduced 2026-10-15: `inv.list({ state: 'local' })` beside `inv.list({ state: 'local', where: { status: 'open' } })` over rows `[1 paid, 2 open, 3 open]` — the first list's `rows` is `[2, 3]` (expected `[1, 2, 3]`) because both subscribe to `resource.store` and the second load replaces it. Seven apps met it (portal, jazzhr, notion, charter, ELA ×2, maid.tech) and all moved to `composed: true`, which `list.js` documents as *the service answers more than the rows*; that spelling re-reads its whole window on every announcement of the service and its rows are not in the store, so `move()` minted a wrong key from it (maid.tech 7c). The red test is the one above, in `test/resource-list.test.js`, which asserts both lists' ids.
  - **A** — a list that states `where` (or its own `query`) keeps its own rows, fed by its `load()` answer and kept live from pushes through `matchesQuery`; a list stating none stays the resource's store. `composed` keeps its meaning (includes). The doc line is one sentence: *a list over a filter is its own.*
  - **B** — `composed: true` is the documented spelling for a second list, renamed or re-described as *own rows*, and `move()` learns to read the key from the list's rows. No schema or live-store change; the cost stays a full window re-read per announcement.
  - **C** — docs only, the row's candidate: sierra's AGENTS.md says a list is its resource's store and names `composed: true` for a second one. Cheapest, and leaves the silent wrong-rows failure for the next app.
  - **Recommend A** — the failure is silent and fires on the natural spelling (jazzhr never meant to write two lists), so the default must be the safe one rather than a flag an app learns after it draws the wrong rows; B/C keep the trap and make every app pay a re-read per announcement.

- **FJS-D1166 — Does `dndzone` grow a copy mode for a palette roster (the dragged item stays in its origin), and is it a flag, a clone function, or the host's reading of the contract?** Found fixing `FJS-2818`. The header of [dnd.js](packages/ui/dnd.js) says both callbacks write the list back, and `finishDrag` hands the origin `itemsWithoutShadow(origin)` with trigger `droppedIntoAnother`, so a roster that keeps its chip loses it from view for the whole drag and holds a hidden gap with the pointer outside every zone. The red test: a drag from a zone declared `copy`, asserting the origin's `onconsider` is never handed a list without the item and its `onfinalize` hands the same rows back; nothing can say that today. A host that ignores the origin's `onconsider` gets the look, but the header promises the opposite and a drop back into the origin has no defined result.
  - **A** — `copy: true` on the source zone. The origin keeps item and element for the whole drag (no shadow, no `onconsider` that removes it), a target zone's `onconsider`/`onfinalize` receive the item as usual, and the origin's `onfinalize` fires with trigger `copiedIntoAnother` and its own rows unchanged; a drop back into the origin cancels. Cost: one option and one trigger; the copy still has the origin item's `id`, so a target `{#each}` keyed on it collides until the host re-ids it in `onfinalize`.
  - **B** — `copy: (item) => record` on the source zone: the function builds the record that travels (fresh `id`, or the Assignment a Person becomes), the placeholder and the ghost stand for it, and the origin is untouched. Also answers the foreign-item half `FJS-D1024` option B left to the host. Cost: the record shape moves into `dnd.js`'s hit-test path, which B of that ruling declined as `accept`; two rulings would then disagree.
  - **C** — nothing in `dnd.js`: the header gains one clause saying a host MAY ignore the origin's `onconsider` and hand the origin's rows back on `onfinalize`, and the palette pattern is documented. Cost: the look rests on a clause no test holds, and the hidden-gap and drop-back cases stay the host's to handle.
  - **Recommend A** — a palette is a zone-level fact the zone can state once, and the origin staying put is what only the shadow logic in `dnd.js` can guarantee; the foreign-record half stays with `FJS-D1024`, whose B already leaves it to the host.

- **FJS-D1167 — Which instant does a fold wall clock in DateTimeInput mean?** On the night clocks fall back, `2026-11-01T01:30` in America/Chicago is two instants (06:30Z CDT, 07:30Z CST). `fromLocalInput` always answers the earlier (`datetime-zone.mjs`: `fromLocalInput('2026-11-01T01:30', {timeZone:'America/Chicago'})` is `06:30:00.000Z`), so a stored CST (later) value shown as 01:30 snaps to CDT the first time the person touches the box, and nobody can enter the later one. The gap half is refused (FJS-3155); the fold survives the round trip so nothing flags it.
  - **A** — Always the earlier instant (today's behavior), documented. The later fold hour cannot be entered.
  - **B** — Keep the value's own offset: when the box's wall clock equals the wall clock of the current value, return that value unchanged; otherwise the earlier. An untouched stored value never moves.
  - **C** — B, plus a local notice ("this time happens twice") with a pick of the two offsets when the typed wall clock is a fold.
  - **Recommend B** — fixes the silent move of an existing value at no new UI; C is a follow-up if a person must author the later hour.

- **FJS-D1168 — How does a Combobox's search box name its field to a `<Form>` once `name` moves to a hidden input?** `FJS-3154`: `Combobox.mesa` puts `name={name}` on the visible search box, so a plain `<form>` submit (`FormData`) carries the typed label and no value. The DatePicker precedent is a hidden `<input name>` carrying the value, with the visible control unnamed. But `Form.mesa` `handleLeave` (blur capture, `e.target.name`) and `handleChange` (`input`/`change`, `e.target.name`) both key on the NAME of the element the event came from; an unnamed search box fires them with `key === undefined`, so a combobox would never reveal its error on blur and typing would not mark it touched (`_touched` is set at Form.mesa ~905 from the key). `FJS-D903` ruled that a commit calls `$context.form.changed(name)`, and Combobox already does; it ruled nothing about leaving a field. Red test: a `<Form validateOn="blur">` with a required `Combobox name="tz"`, focus then blur the search box without picking, assert the field's error shows and `new FormData(formEl).get('tz')` is the picked value, not the label.
  - **A** — the search box carries `data-field={name}` and Form's two listeners read `e.target.name || e.target.dataset.field`. Nothing for a control to call; any future composite with a visible unnamed box states the same attribute. The price is a second spelling of "this element belongs to field X" that a hand-written control must know.
  - **B** — the control calls the owner, `D903`'s shape: `form.changed(name)` already exists and a sibling `form.left(name)` is called from Combobox's `handleBlur`. `handleLeave` becomes a caller of it. No attribute and no event inference; a control outside a form calls nothing. A control that forgets is silent, so it owes the same artefact `D903` named, a drive that blurs each control inside a hand-written `<Form>`.
  - **C** — the hidden input carries `name` and the search box keeps an `aria`-only identity; Form resolves a blur from the search box by walking to the nearest `.field-group` and reading its hidden `[name]`. Needs no change to the control but makes Form reason about markup it does not own.
  - **Recommend B** — it is the owner shape `D903` already ruled for the same family of events (Invariant 4), `form.changed` is in place, and A/C make Form infer a field from DOM it does not own. The fix then also moves DatePicker, MultiSelect and the other event-less controls onto `left` in one pass rather than each growing its own naming trick.

- **FJS-D1169 — Should a stated `disabled={false}` on a Button beat a disabled Form, as it does on every other control?** `Button.mesa` defaults `disabled` to `false` and computes `isOff = disabled || form.disabled`, so nothing a caller states can open a button inside a disabled form; every other control defaults `undefined` and resolves `stated(disabled, form?.disabled)`. Its comment calls the bypass deliberate: a disabled form is the caller saying the whole form is off, including the way out.
  - **A** — `disabled` defaults `undefined` and a submit/other button resolves `stated(disabled, form?.disabled)` like the rest, so a Cancel stays live on a form whose fields are locked by saying `disabled={false}`. A `type="submit"` button still follows `committed` and `submitting`.
  - **B** — keep the bypass and document it as the one exception; a way out of a locked form is the caller's own button outside the `<Form>`.
  - **Recommend A** — one resolution rule for every control, and the deliberate case is still reachable by stating nothing.

- ~~**FJS-D1170 — When an autosave Form is destroyed inside its quiet window, does it send the waiting edit or warn that it dropped it?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-17 (`FJS-D1170`): A — `onDestroy` flushes: if a timer is armed, send the write once more (fire and forget; `onautosaved` is not called on a destroyed form).** `$.onDestroy` clears `_autoTimer`, so closing a drawer 300 ms after the last key loses the edit with no message; `flushAutosave()` exists but only a caller that remembers it saves the edit.
  - **A** — `onDestroy` flushes: if a timer is armed, send the write once more (fire and forget; `onautosaved` is not called on a destroyed form).
  - **B** — `onDestroy` drops the edit and `console.warn`s that a pending autosave was discarded, naming the model, so the screen owner calls `flushAutosave()`.
  - **Recommend A** — the quiet window exists so the person never presses save; losing the last edit on close is the one failure a form with no button cannot afford.

- **FJS-D1171 — How does a sized Button, Badge, Pill, Kbd or Mono reach a font-size an app class can override?** `FJS-3159`: each writes `style:font-size={fontSize}` (`Button.mesa:75,95`, `Badge.mesa:41`, `Pill.mesa:37`, `Kbd.mesa:27,31`, `Mono.mesa:17`), and an inline declaration beats any app class, so `.my-btn { font-size: … }` loses on a `size="xs"` button. Red test: render `<Button size="xs">` and assert the output carries no `style=` font-size. `@frontierjs/css` says no `.btn-sm` (`vocabulary.js` density note) and no `.sm`/`.lg` on Avatar, so the row's "a size class in css" has no home yet.
  - **A** — a fifth axis in css, `size`: free-standing `.xs .sm .lg` (a `NOT_A_TERM` group like tone and density) setting `font-size: var(--text-*)` on anything chip-shaped; the components write `class="xs"` and drop the inline style. One axis named once, but maximally generic class names — the liability `avatar.css` already declines.
  - **B** — a data attribute, `[data-size="xs"]` per component in `buttons.css`, `badges.css`, `pills.css`, `code.css`: no new vocabulary term and no collision with a tone or modifier, but one rule block per component and the size is not a class an author can write by hand.
  - **C** — a token, `--chip-font-size`, that each component's CSS reads (`font-size: var(--chip-font-size, var(--text-xs))`) and the component sets by a class `size-xs`; an app overrides the token or the font-size, and both lose to nothing inline.
  - **Recommend A** — the density axis is the precedent for one axis named once, `xs sm lg` mirror the `--text-*` ladder, and a class is what an app class composes with; the generic-name risk is answered by scoping the rules to chip-shaped elements only.

- **FJS-D1172 — Does FileUpload get a way to not list its files, and a zone small enough to hold a picture?** `FileUpload` keeps every file and lists each under the zone, with no prop to turn the list off; emptying `bind:files` to hide it is lost inside `onchange` (`FJS-2509`). Darkroom's Batch queue listed twelve pictures twice, and its Before / After slots (70 px) fell back to a local button because the zone has one size (`FJS-2474`, which fixed the wording and the unrevoked preview and left this).
  - **A** — two props on `FileUpload`: `list={false}` (the caller draws the files) and `compact` (a zone sized to its box, showing the first image chosen as its face).
  - **B** — one prop, `list={false}`, and a caller wanting a small zone styles the button through `{...$attributes}` / `class`.
  - **C** — a second component (`FilePick`) for the no-list, small case; `FileUpload` stays the dropzone with a list.
  - **Recommend B** — a hidden list is the only thing a caller cannot do from outside; size is already reachable through `class` and the zone's own tokens, so a second prop buys no capability.

- **FJS-D1173 — How does a Block say a solid tone fill: a new treatment on the surface, a header-only modifier, or a toned header that fills by default?** Found fixing `FJS-2560`. `surface.css` derives `--surface-tint-bg` from `--tint-surface`, so `.card.success .surface-header` is a 10%-style tint; the contrast-capped solid derivation (`--_fill` / `--_on-fill`, from `--tone-fill`) lives only in `chip.css`'s `:where(...)` list, which is inline-flex chip layout. Red assertion (not committed): `<div class="card success"><div class="surface-header">` — header background must be the tone fill (equal to a `.pill.success` background) and its text AA against it in every theme; today it is the tint. Any answer adds vocabulary (`New vocabulary is a design decision`) and `FJS-2687`'s ribbon and `FJS-D1163`'s record page lean on the same fill.
  - **A** — a treatment `solid` on the Block tier (`.card.success.solid`): the whole surface paints `--_fill` with `--_on-fill` ink and its own border, next to `raised`/`outlined`/`ghost`/`glass`, listed in `treatment` and the wizard. Orthogonal and composes with tone; a header band alone then needs a nested card or `.band.solid`, and a solid card body is rarely wanted (long text on a saturated fill).
  - **B** — the fill is a Block sub-region rule, not a treatment: `.surface-header.solid` (or `.card > .surface-header.solid`) paints the tone the surface already carries. Says exactly what the row reports (a header band) and leaves the body readable, but a treatment listed on the sub-region is the "wrong ELEMENT" trap in `CLAUDE.md`, and it needs an anatomy entry rather than a vocabulary treatment.
  - **C** — no modifier: a toned Card's `.surface-header` fills solid by default. Nothing new to learn, but it changes every toned card in the kit and the apps (`example`, `basecamp`) at once, and drops the tint header a quiet success/danger card relies on.
  - **Recommend A** — a treatment is what vocabulary already calls an orthogonal change of fill, and the derivation to reuse (`chip.css`) is a fill-plus-ink pair that wants a whole surface as its subject; the header band falls out of `.band.solid` or a `solid` Card with a flat body, and B/C each break a standing rule (element-scoped modifier; silent restyle of existing cards).

- **FJS-D1174 — What noun and shape does the kit's calendar take: a new Calendar component, a grid DatePicker shares, or a css pattern only?** Found fixing `FJS-2826`. `DatePicker.mesa` builds its month as a `<table class="fjs-dp-grid">` of picker cells (`fjs-dp-day`, `fjs-dp-square`) with the cursor, outside dates, today and `startOfWeek` all inside a 1,000-line picker; no kit surface holds an app's entries per date, and three apps (eventmark `MonthGrid.mesa`, sstime `.week`, calendly `Booking.mesa`) each write a `repeat(7)` grid. The red test's assertion: `<Calendar month="2025-08" entries={...}>` renders 6 or fewer week rows of 7 cells, outside dates marked, today marked, and two entries on `2025-08-25` land in ONE cell.
  - **A** — a new `Calendar` component in `packages/ui/components/display/`: props `month` (cursor, `bind:`-able), `startOfWeek`, `entries` (array, each with a date key), a per-date slot receiving `{date, entries, outside, today}`, `onselect(date)`; month view only, a week view is `weeks={1}`. DatePicker is left alone, so the two keep separate day math.
  - **B** — extract the month math (cursor, weeks padded with outside dates, today) into a `@frontierjs/toolbelt` kit function and a `Calendar` component over it; DatePicker's `buildCurrentPeriod`/`dowLabels`/`startOfWeek` move onto the same function, so one owner computes a month (Invariant 4).
  - **C** — css only: a `.calendar` pattern in `@frontierjs/css` (`repeat(7)` grid, `.outside`, `.today` and a cell's height) that apps fill by hand; no component, no month math.
  - **Recommend B** — the row names the cursor, outside dates and today, which DatePicker already computes, so A writes the second month calculation; C leaves the per-date entries slot, the part three apps hand-rolled, to each app.

- **FJS-D1175 — How does Tabs step to the next or previous tab: a `TabStep` child, a prop on Tabs, or a public `adjacent()` on the context?** Found fixing `FJS-2678`. `Tabs.mesa` holds the selectable-tab registry and `adjacentId`/`edgeId` (`Tabs.mesa:51,70`), but `adjacentId` wraps (it serves ArrowLeft/Right) and both are reachable only by a `TabList`/`Tab` descendant through `$context`; a form split into tabs (ela's survey editor, four tabs) writes its own two buttons over its own id list, so its order can drift from the strip and it steps onto disabled tabs. Red assertion: in a `<Tabs>` of general / scale / email, a "next" control at `scale` activates `email`, at `email` is disabled or absent (no wrap to `general`), and a tab with `disabled` is skipped.
  - **A** — a child component `TabStep` (`dir="next" | "prev"`, slot is the label, forwards `$attributes`) used inside `<Tabs>` anywhere, e.g. under the panels: it reads `$context.adjacent`, does not wrap (disabled at the end), and sets `activeId` through `setActive`. The app writes no id list and chooses layout and label; adds one noun to the Tabs family.
  - **B** — a prop on `Tabs`, `showStepper` (the `DatePicker` precedent, `DatePicker.mesa:113`): Tabs draws a Prev/Next pair after the panels itself. No new noun, but the labels, placement and a Finish button on the last step are all the form's to choose, and a prop per variation follows.
  - **C** — publish what exists: document `$context.adjacent`/`edge` and expose a non-wrapping `next()`/`prev()` pair so an app's own buttons call them. Smallest surface, but a Mesa app outside `<Tabs>` has no handle on a context, so it works only inside it, which is `A` without the component.
  - **Recommend A** — the strip already owns the order and the disabled-skipping (`Tabs.mesa` registry), so a child that reads it keeps one owner of "what is next" while the app keeps layout and wording; B fixes a layout the form wants to own.

- ~~**FJS-D1199 — How does a broadcast learn who could read an updated row BEFORE the write?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-17 (`FJS-D1199`): A — Litestone's `WriteEvent` gains `before` (the raw pre-write row, in-process only like `where`), read when a `$tapEvents` tap or the emitter is listening (`hasAudience()`), and used by both junction paths: `gradeRecipients` takes an optional `before` and sends `removed` only to a cohort that read `before` and was refused `after`. A row-scoped write with no `before` (cross-process, which stores ids; `select: false`; bulk `rows` mode) sends no removal, failing closed. Cost: one extra SELECT per tapped single-row update, and bulk `announce: 'rows'` reads N rows; a cross-process write can no longer revoke a row from a store live.** `gradeRecipients` (`packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts`, the `if (!visible) { if (removal) …` branch) sends `<svc> removed <id>` to every cohort `$readAs` refuses on the after-image, so a guest or another tenant's socket hears the id and moment of every update (FJS-1742). Grading the before-image too is the fix the row names, but no layer hands it over: Litestone's `WriteEvent` carries `result` only, its `beforeRow` exists for logged / post-update-rule models alone and is the REDACTED trail image, and the service path's `announce(event, data, ctx, fn)` has only the after row. Red test: a guest cohort refused both images receives no `orders removed` frame (today: one frame, `{ id }`).
  - **A** — Litestone's `WriteEvent` gains `before` (the raw pre-write row, in-process only like `where`), read when a `$tapEvents` tap or the emitter is listening (`hasAudience()`), and used by both junction paths: `gradeRecipients` takes an optional `before` and sends `removed` only to a cohort that read `before` and was refused `after`. A row-scoped write with no `before` (cross-process, which stores ids; `select: false`; bulk `rows` mode) sends no removal, failing closed. Cost: one extra SELECT per tapped single-row update, and bulk `announce: 'rows'` reads N rows; a cross-process write can no longer revoke a row from a store live.
  - **B** — As A, but a write with no `before` keeps today's behavior (removal to every refused reader), so only a write that supplies a before-image is narrowed. Never loses a row from a live store; leaves the oracle open on cross-process and bulk writes, which is where the quo ring and chatwoot widget cases write (`asSystem().updateMany`, `announce: 'rows'`), so FJS-1742's amendments stay open.
  - **C** — No before-image: send `removed` only to a cohort that the after-image refuses AND that is a member of a channel the row's TENANT/room claims (the claim says the row's neighborhood). Closes the cross-tenant and widget-visitor amendments with no extra read, but a guest of the same workspace still hears every page edit, which is the row's headline case.
  - **Recommend A** — it is the only one that closes all three reports (guest, tenant, bearer visitor), the before-read is already paid for by `hasAudience()` gating, and failing closed on an unknown before matches how `gradeRecipients` already treats an undecidable `$readAs` ("refuse, never widen").

- ~~**FJS-D1200 — How does the login limiter bound a spray without refusing a room behind one address?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-17 (`FJS-D1200`): A — Add `loginAccountRateLimit` (default `{ max: 10, window: '15 minutes' }`), a second bucket keyed on the email in the body, checked after the body is read; `loginRateLimit` stays the per-address bucket and its default rises (say 100 per 15 minutes) to be the looser one. One new option, two defaults, and `/login/challenge` keys on its ticket's account or stays per-address only.** Found fixing FJS-3128: `loginRateLimit` is one bucket keyed on the caller's IP (`packages/auth/plugin.ts` `loginLimiter`, shared by `/login`, `/login/challenge` and `/magic-link/verify`), so 50 students on one school address get a 429 from the eleventh, and raising `max` to the room size also stops guarding a spray from that address. Red test: 11 logins for 11 distinct emails from one `ctx.ip` under the default answer 429 on the eleventh; wanted, all 11 pass while 11 wrong passwords for ONE email still refuse.
  - **A** — Add `loginAccountRateLimit` (default `{ max: 10, window: '15 minutes' }`), a second bucket keyed on the email in the body, checked after the body is read; `loginRateLimit` stays the per-address bucket and its default rises (say 100 per 15 minutes) to be the looser one. One new option, two defaults, and `/login/challenge` keys on its ticket's account or stays per-address only.
  - **B** — `loginRateLimit` accepts a list of buckets, each `{ max, window, key? }`, so the plugin loops them. No second option name, but a number-or-array union on one option and the email key must be a named helper an app imports.
  - **C** — No framework change: document that `loginRateLimit.key` already takes a function and that an app wanting a per-account bucket writes it in the awaited `onLogin` hook. The room's workaround (`max: 120`) stays the default experience and a spray from a shared address is unguarded.
  - **Recommend A** — the register's own correction names exactly this (a per-account key that lets the per-address cap loosen), `reauthenticationRateLimit` is the precedent for a sibling limiter option, and B/C leave every app to rediscover the 429 at its first classroom.

- ~~**FJS-D1201 — Who ends an open socket whose session is revoked or whose principal changes after the upgrade?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-17 (`FJS-D1201`): B — The transport re-verifies: the socket keeps its upgrade credential, and `IAuth.verifySession` is asked again on every incoming frame (HTTP's cost per request) and on an interval for a socket that only receives. A refused answer or a changed principal closes the socket through `disconnectSession`'s path, and the reconnect re-resolves it. One owner covers revocation, expiry, level change and a write from another process, but it adds one number (the interval), and a push-only socket stays stale for up to that interval.** Found fixing FJS-3187: a socket resolves its principal once at upgrade (`channels.ts` `disconnectSession` comment), and `disconnectSession` has two callers, both support mode (`packages/auth/plugin.ts` `closeSockets`). `auth.ts` deletes Session rows at eight sites (`logout`, `revokeSession`, `revokeSessions`, `changePassword`, the password reset, the TOTP reset, the OAuth link, and the delete at `auth.ts:1866`) and none closes a socket; `FJS-D548` closes only a cookie socket of the browser whose own response changed the cookie. A role or `sessionFields` change is an app's write to the User row, which auth never sees, and an expired session's socket is never closed either. The red test: upgrade with a bearer subprotocol, `auth.revokeSession` from a second session, and assert the first socket closes. Today it stays open and goes on receiving graded broadcasts as the old principal.
  - **A** — Auth closes at each edge: one helper in `auth.ts` reads the ids of the sessions a write is about to delete and hands them to a closer, which the plugin wires to `app.channels.disconnectSession`, the same as support mode. Every one of the eight delete sites goes through it. The close is immediate for every revocation auth performs, push included, but a level change, an expiry and a delete made by another process are not covered.
  - **B** — The transport re-verifies: the socket keeps its upgrade credential, and `IAuth.verifySession` is asked again on every incoming frame (HTTP's cost per request) and on an interval for a socket that only receives. A refused answer or a changed principal closes the socket through `disconnectSession`'s path, and the reconnect re-resolves it. One owner covers revocation, expiry, level change and a write from another process, but it adds one number (the interval), and a push-only socket stays stale for up to that interval.
  - **C** — Both: A for the edges auth writes, so logout and revocation close at once, and B's interval for the changes auth cannot see.
  - **Recommend B** — the principal already has one owner (`verifySession`), and asking it again is the only design that covers the changes auth never writes (role, `sessionFields`, expiry). A covers only the edges auth writes and leaves the level-change half of the row open; C states the same close in two places.

- ~~**FJS-D1204 — How does a template's sender form check a typed value: does `mergeFields` validate it, or does it hand Sierra's existing field rules a schema to grade?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-18 (`FJS-D1204`): B — `mergeFields` grows `schema(filter?)` → a JSON Schema object (`number` → `{ type: 'number' }`, `date` → `{ type: 'string', format: 'date' }`, `default` → `default`), and the form is `createResource`/`Form` over it, so `buildFieldRules`, `coerceToSchema` and `controlFor` (Invariant 4's owners of translation and control choice) grade and draw it. Toolbelt adds no validator; the cost is that `money`'s value shape is still to rule (a number of the currency's major unit, as `@money` in litestone, is the precedent).** Found fixing `FJS-2684`. `FJS-D980` and `FJS-2683` are built: `mergeFields(declared).with(rowFields)` gives the form its fields (`list()` carries `label`, `type`, `default`) and `context(data)` carries a value into `fill` with defaults laid under a missing one. What is absent is the check that a sender's value fits its `type` (`text | textarea | number | date | money`) before it reaches the merge: `mergeFields({ key: 'quote.deposit', type: 'number', label: 'Deposit' }).context({ quote: { deposit: 'abc' } })` fills `abc` with no error. Checking forces two choices the register leaves open: what `money` and `date` values look like, and whether a form's string is coerced (`'12'` → `12`) or refused.
  - **A** — `mergeFields` grows `values(input, filter?)` → `{ values, errors }`: `number` and `money` a finite number (a numeric string is coerced), `date` an ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` string, `text`/`textarea` a string; `errors` is `{ 'owner.field': message }`. The sender's UI draws `list()` with `@frontierjs/ui`'s controls and calls `values` on submit. One owner for the catalog and its check; toolbelt now carries a second small validator beside Sierra's `buildFieldRules`.
  - **B** — `mergeFields` grows `schema(filter?)` → a JSON Schema object (`number` → `{ type: 'number' }`, `date` → `{ type: 'string', format: 'date' }`, `default` → `default`), and the form is `createResource`/`Form` over it, so `buildFieldRules`, `coerceToSchema` and `controlFor` (Invariant 4's owners of translation and control choice) grade and draw it. Toolbelt adds no validator; the cost is that `money`'s value shape is still to rule (a number of the currency's major unit, as `@money` in litestone, is the precedent).
  - **C** — a template row's fields are an `@@extensible` model (`FJS-D487`): the declared list lives in the schema, litestone validates the value on write and Junction's `declaredFields` method feeds the form. Nothing new in toolbelt, but `mergeFields` stops being the catalog for a template row and `FJS-2683`'s second source becomes a third.
  - **Recommend B** — Sierra already owns *what a typed form checks and draws*; A would be a second validator for the same five types, and C moves a built catalog into a model before the sender form exists.

- **FJS-D1234 — How does `rank()` stop rebuilding its index per keystroke: a cache keyed on the items array, an exported prebuilt ranker the callers hold, or nothing?** Found fixing `FJS-2226`. The plain path of `rank` (`packages/toolbelt/src/search/search.js:104`) does `new QuickScore(items, …)`, whose constructor runs `setItems` and lowercases every string of every item, then discards it; `rankWords` lowercases per field per call too. Measured by the row at 1k/10k/50k items: 4.2/22/96 ms through `rank` against 2.2/15/62 ms prebuilt. `CommandPalette.mesa:138` and `MultiSelect.mesa:228` call it per keystroke; MultiSelect builds `Object.values(optionsMap)` fresh each time, so only a caller holding a stable list can ever reuse anything. The red test: assert the corpus is folded once across two `rank` calls on one list, which fails today because each call folds it again.
  - **A** — `rank` keeps a `WeakMap` from the items array (then `keys` and `minimumScore`) to its QuickScore. No new name and callers need no change, but a list edited in place is answered from a stale index, MultiSelect's fresh array never hits until it holds one, and `rankWords` still needs its own cache.
  - **B** — `/search` exports `createRanker(items, { keys, minimumScore })` returning `{ rank(query, opts) }`, with `rank()` a one-shot over it. The index has one owner and an edit is a new ranker, so nothing goes stale. It coins one noun, and CommandPalette and MultiSelect each hold a ranker in a derived const over their items.
  - **C** — No change: the cost is the lowercasing that any non-held index must pay, 4 ms per keystroke at 1k items, and a picker of that size is already past what a person scans. Close `FJS-2226` as measured and leave the 50k case to a caller who pre-filters.
  - **Recommend B** — a cache keyed on identity is the staleness `rankWords`' and the palette's in-place edits would meet first, while a held ranker moves the cost to where the list changes rather than where it is typed against; C is right only if no picker is expected past a few thousand rows, and `resource.options()` fills one from database rows.

- **FJS-D1235 — Which motion words does the kit add for a thing that MOVES?** Found fixing `FJS-2257`. `tokens.css:249` has four durations (120–200 ms) and three easings (`ease`, `ease-out`, `--motion-ease-enter`), all for arriving, fading or a control changing shape; a view transition (a window growing, a dock sliding) therefore declares its own `--stage-spring`, `--stage-move`, `--stage-morph` on `:root`, which Invariant 13 calls a word the kit should own. The row fixes neither the names nor the values, and Portal's PLAN (`../fjs-prototypes/portal`) is not in this tree to read them from. Red test once ruled: `theming.spec.js` (beside the FJS-162 motion block, `:391`) asserts each new token resolves at `:root` and that a theme retuning it moves a rule using it; `vocabulary.spec.js` fails until the new names are in `vocabulary.js`.
  - **A** — three tokens mirroring Portal: `--motion-spring` (an easing, a `linear()` overshoot curve), `--motion-move` (a duration, ~400 ms, something travelling) and `--motion-morph` (a duration, ~600 ms, one box becoming another), beside `--motion-slow` in `tokens.css`. Portal's three words survive as written, but two durations and one easing are three rungs with no second user in the repo, and the values are Portal's, not measured here.
  - **B** — one duration and one easing: `--motion-travel` (a thing moving across the screen, ~400 ms) and `--motion-ease-spring`, matching the existing `--motion-<job>` / `--motion-ease-<job>` split. A morph is a `travel` at the call site; two tokens instead of three, and the easing joins the family `--motion-ease*` already names. Portal renames its three uses.
  - **C** — nothing: a view transition is an app's choreography, not the kit's, and `--stage-*` on `:root` is already the right owner. Close `FJS-2257` as declined and add the one sentence to `CLAUDE.md` § Motion so the next stressor does not refile it.
  - **Recommend B** — the kit's motion words are named for the JOB and its easings already live in `--motion-ease-*`, so B keeps the family predictable from one rung to the next; A adds a duration per Portal scene before a second app has asked, and C leaves Invariant 13's gap open for every app that animates a view transition. Whichever lands, `prefers-reduced-motion` must also cover `::view-transition-*`, which the `*` guard at `tokens.css:815` does not reach.

- **FJS-D1236 — What is a star rating in the kit: a css component over native radios, a Mesa component in ui, or nothing?** Found fixing `FJS-2112`. Ela's public survey draws a 1–5 answer as stars from native radios plus a scoped `<style>` block, and a read-only `Stars.mesa` repeats the drawing; the css vocabulary (`vocabulary.js`) has no term for either, so the fix coins a noun and picks its owner. The red test, once the owner is chosen: the vocabulary spec fails until the class is named, and a `.rating` radio group's checked input fills every star up to it.
  - **A** — `@frontierjs/css` grows `.rating` in `src/components/`: a `fieldset`/`div` of visually hidden radios and their labels, filled by `input:checked ~ label` over a reversed flex row, with `.rating.readonly` (or `[data-value]`) for the display and tone from the existing `tone` axis. Pure CSS, no script, named in `vocabulary.js`; the survey keeps native radios, so form posts and keyboard arrows work unchanged.
  - **B** — `@frontierjs/ui` grows `Rating.mesa` (props `value`, `max`, `readonly`, `name`) that renders the radios and carries its own scoped style. It is one file and the read-only display is the same component, but a style block in a kit package ships what Invariant 13 says the kit should state in `@frontierjs/css`, and a plain-HTML or email consumer gets nothing.
  - **C** — Nothing in the kit: a star is a glyph an app draws, and ela's scoped block is the right owner. Close `FJS-2112` as declined and add the one sentence to the css `CLAUDE.md` so the next survey does not ask again.
  - **Recommend A** — the drawing is style over a native control, which is what `@frontierjs/css` owns and what B would re-own in markup; A also gives the read-only display and the input one class, where C leaves two copies in every app that asks a rating.

- ~~**FJS-D1212 — Which schemas does `FJS-D1206`'s deny-by-default reach: every schema, a schema declaring any access rule, or a schema declaring a `@@gate`?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-18 (`FJS-D1212`): C — A schema that declares any access rule, using `schemaDeclaresAccessRules`, the same per-schema line `FJS-D52` draws for raw SQL (`@@gate`, `@@allow`, `@@deny`, `@guarded`, `@encrypted`, `@secret`, a field `@allow`, `@scoped`, `@system`). A schema with none is a plain ORM and stays open.** Found fixing `FJS-3193`. `FJS-D1206` says a model with no gate and no policy refuses everything below the system principal, but `GatePlugin` is installed only when a schema declares a `@@gate` (`packages/litestone/src/core/client.js:11058`), so a schema with no gates runs ungraded and the ruling does not say whether that changes. Built and measured: `buildAccessMap` (`packages/litestone/src/plugins/gate.js:139`) gives an undeclared model `{8,8,8,8}`, and the refusal reads *Note declares no @@gate and no @@allow/@@deny, so only asSystem() reaches it. Declare who may — @@gate(0) makes it public*. The red test: on `model Note { id String @id  body String }` beside a `@@gate(0)` model, `db.$setAuth(null).note.create(...)` resolves today and must throw. The root client grades as a stranger, so every bare `db.item.create` in a test of such a schema refuses too. The litestone suite fails 179 tests under B and 615 under C, against none today. The ruling's measurement (0 of 114 app models ungated) counted apps and not test fixtures or docs fences (`test/doc-examples.test.ts` fails 56 under B).
  - **A** — Every schema. `GatePlugin` is always installed, so a schema with no access declarations at all refuses every caller below `asSystem()`, the root client included. This is the ruling read literally and ZenStack's enhanced client. Litestone stops working as a plain ORM without `asSystem()`, and most of the 7,600 litestone tests change.
  - **B** — A schema that declares a `@@gate` (the existing auto-install line). One gated model makes every ungated, unpolicied model beside it SYSTEM-only. A schema whose only access rule is an `@@allow` leaves its other models open.
  - **C** — A schema that declares any access rule, using `schemaDeclaresAccessRules`, the same per-schema line `FJS-D52` draws for raw SQL (`@@gate`, `@@allow`, `@@deny`, `@guarded`, `@encrypted`, `@secret`, a field `@allow`, `@scoped`, `@system`). A schema with none is a plain ORM and stays open.
  - **Recommend C**: `FJS-D52` already decides per schema that "this schema declares access rules" is the line past which the ORM refuses to run unguarded. C reuses that predicate rather than drawing a second one. B leaves an `@@allow`-only schema's other models open, which is the defect this row names.
  The second half, a model-less custom method with no `gate:`, follows from the same reading. Its `unchecked` grade in `customMethodGrade` (`packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts:3255`) refuses every caller except an internal call with no principal, which is what `isSystem: !call?.auth?.user` (`litestone.ts:5027`) already treats as the application acting for itself.

- ~~**FJS-D1224 — Where does the app-level browser drive live, and what does a verify file call?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-18 (`FJS-D1224`): A — split by owner, one call: `waitFor`, `fill`, `shot` and a `failed` list of responses of 400 and above grow `@frontierjs/mesa/drive`, the page's owner by `FJS-D554`; `@frontierjs/testing/drive` is `drive(options, steps)`, owning the port refusal (`FJS-740`), each server as a process group, the checks and the exit code. `mesa` is an optional peer of testing.** Found fixing `FJS-1563`. Four app copies (linear, connectteam, maidtech, transit) each carry a start-poll-kill wrapper and page helpers, and `@frontierjs/mesa/drive`'s `errors` collected no failed response.
  - **A** — split by owner, one call: `waitFor`, `fill`, `shot` and a `failed` list of responses of 400 and above grow `@frontierjs/mesa/drive`, the page's owner by `FJS-D554`; `@frontierjs/testing/drive` is `drive(options, steps)`, owning the port refusal (`FJS-740`), each server as a process group, the checks and the exit code. `mesa` is an optional peer of testing.
  - **B** — both halves in `@frontierjs/testing/drive`, as the row proposed. The failed-response list and `waitFor` sit beside mesa's `errors`, a second owner, and mesa, ui and css drives cannot reach them (`FJS-D554`'s reason against `testing/browser`).
  - **C** — a new `@frontierjs/drive` package over mesa's, `FJS-D554`'s option B. The same code plus a package and a noun.
  - **Recommend A** — each half sits with the owner it already has, and one call is the paved road: a verify file that composes building blocks re-writes the try/finally and the signal kill, which is what the four copies are.

- **FJS-D1232 — What does a `///` doc comment on its own line with no member after it (last in a model, trait, type, extend, view or enum body) do?** `parse('model A {\n  id Int @id\n  /// note\n}')` is `valid: false` — `Unexpected token '}' inside model 'A'` — and the view and enum loops fail the same way (`Expected IDENT, got '}'`); `FJS-2386` took only the `///` on the line a field ENDS on. The same-line `@@index([id]) /// t` fails too, since a model attribute carries no comments.
  - **A** — drop it: the comment is read and attaches to nothing, so a trailing note parses everywhere. The parser's one loop-top `docComments()` already reads it; the body loop skips the member branch when `}` follows. A note silently lost is the cost, and `//` is the spelling for a note that documents nothing.
  - **B** — attach it to the member above, as `trailingComments` does for a same-line `///`. A reader who writes it after the last field means that field, but a `///` on its own line leads the NEXT member everywhere else, so one spelling means two things by position.
  - **C** — keep the refusal and name it: `A /// doc comment documents the member after it; this one ends model 'A' and documents nothing — use //`. Nothing is lost silently and the mistake is found at the line, at the cost of every body loop gaining the check.
  - **Recommend C** — a doc comment is data that reaches the JSON Schema and the types (`FJS-D204`), so dropping one without a word is a documentation loss nothing reports, while naming it costs one message.
- **FJS-D1237 — Is the status ribbon a new Ribbon term bleeding by the Card's own padding rung, a part of the record-page aside FJS-2686 has not drawn yet, or a Badge shape?** FJS-2687 asks for a filled band, status-toned, rounded on the end only, bleeding to the container's start edge. The row says the kit has only Pill, but Pill is the count and Badge is the categorical status (`vocabulary.js`, `badges.css`); the missing thing is the bleed, not the status word. How far to bleed is the container's padding, and the one precedent (`.card > .surface-header`, `cards.css:56-65`) writes it as the card's own `--space-3xl` rung. No aside exists in this kit yet (FJS-2686 is open), so a ribbon there has no padding to read. Nine questions: another origin — yes if the aside later restates its padding; concept budget — one new noun; derived — the bleed is the container's padding, so derivable only where a container states one; owner — `cards.css` owns the Card bleed; silence — a ribbon whose bleed drifts from its container's padding renders fine, so a spec must measure the offset at two densities.
  - **A** — New `Ribbon` term (`<span class="ribbon success">`) in `patterns/`, bleeding as `.card > .ribbon` by the Card's padding rung, same rule shape as `.surface-header`. Tone fill and derived ink come from chip.css, end-only radius. The aside later adds its own one-line rule when FJS-2686 lands.
  - **B** — Hold the row for FJS-2686 and make Ribbon a part of the record-page aside (`.aside > .ribbon`), so the padding it bleeds by is stated once, by the aside that owns it.
  - **C** — No new noun: a Badge bleeds through a knob (`.badge.bleed`, reading a `--bleed-inline` the container sets), so the shape is a Badge treatment and Ribbon stays a word in guides only.
  - **Recommend A** — it has a working precedent for the bleed, ships the shape now, and costs one noun the row already names; B waits on an unplanned layout and C makes every container publish a knob.
- **FJS-D1238 — Does a per-user, server-kept UI preference get a framework home, or is it an ordinary app model?** `persisted(key, fallback)` from `@frontierjs/mesa/browser` (`packages/mesa/src/browser/persisted.js`, `FJS-D1097`) already answers FJS-2007's per-viewer half: a rail's open state, a snoozed-shown flag and a filter-token set each survive a reload in one call, prefixed per app. What it cannot do is follow a user across devices, and nothing in the tree names a home for that — an app writes its own `UserPreference` model, service and load-at-boot today.
  - **A** — No new noun: a preference that must follow the user is an app model with an `owner`-scoped row policy (Invariant 6), and `persisted()` takes an optional `{ sync }` seam later only if a second app writes the same load/save glue. Docs: one paragraph in sierra's README § preferences naming both halves.
  - **B** — Auth ships a `preferences` service and model fragment (`prefs: Json` on the user's own row, gate `self`), and `persisted(key, fallback, { scope: 'user' })` reads and writes it through the client, falling back to localStorage when signed out.
  - **C** — Hold: close FJS-2007 on `persisted()` alone and open the server half only when an app (ELA) names the cross-device need.
  - ~~**Recommend C**~~ (superseded 2026-10-28, below) — the row's evidence (ELA's inbox) is per-viewer on one device, which `persisted()` covers; a server scope adds a schema fragment and a sign-in/out merge rule that no app has asked for yet.
  - **Amended 2026-10-28** — the home EXISTS and the question missed it: `@scoped` (and `@edge(ref:)`), `packages/litestone/docs/edge-fields.md`. A per-viewer field on a managed side table keyed `(row, viewer)`, bound from `auth()`, read as `row.mine.<field>`, filterable (`where: { mine: { readAt: null } }` is *unread, per viewer*), promoted to a real model by `litestone edge eject`. Its § *Seen by a person* is `readAt DateTime? @scoped` verbatim. The owner's framing — *a per-user attachment on a row, extra data not a model* — is that attribute's own definition. What the tree holds instead: three app spellings beside it (`Notification.readAt` column, `NotificationPreference` model, `TagAttachment` pair) plus a fourth, `AlertEvent.acknowledgedBy/At` (one acknowledger for everyone), and ZERO schemas using `@scoped` — 31 litestone test hits, no app, no prototype. A `Mark` reference, proposed earlier this day and struck, would have been the fifth spelling (origin fail).
  - **D** — `@scoped` is the home; nothing new in litestone. What is owed is the BRIDGE: `packages/litestone/src/jsonschema.js` names neither `edge` nor `scoped`, so the `mine` namespace is absent from the emitted create/update schema junction validates against — a browser write of `mine` today is refused or stripped under deny-by-default (`FJS-D1206`), which of the two unmeasured — and the filter bridge (`toolbelt/query`, not `directives`: `mine` carries no `$`, Invariant 10) does not admit `where.mine`. Reachable from service code through `ctx.db` now; from the browser through generated CRUD after the bridge. Filed as a row below.
  - **Recommend D, with the first instance in basecamp** — basecamp's notices (`lib/notices.js`, derived from server/deploy/job state) become occurrences a person marks: **D-A** a notice IS an `AlertEvent` (already one row per firing, `subjectType`/`subjectId`), and `acknowledgedBy/At` becomes `readAt DateTime? @scoped`; **D-B** an own `Notice` model with the same `@scoped` field beside `AlertEvent` — a second occurrence log, so say why a notice is not an alert before taking it. Recommend D-A. An occurrence row answers the derived-id problem for free: a recurring condition is a new row, so it re-shows. Condition (a) stands: the build strikes `NoticeBar.mesa:9` (*deliberately not persisted*), and the filter lives once in `lib/notices.js` — `NoticeBar` and `ActionQueue` each hold their own `dismissed = []`. `persisted()` stays per-viewer for a rail or a filter set (FJS-2007); B is still unasked for. Map work the ruling does not do: a pointer in `data-hazards` or litestone's `CLAUDE.md` — *per-viewer attachment → `@scoped`* — which is how this stayed unused through three app spellings.

- **FJS-D1239 — Do the colour control, the list/detail layout and the time-zone picker each get a new component, or does the kit grow them as treatments of `Input`, `@frontierjs/css` and `Combobox`?** Found fixing `FJS-1981`, three gaps in one row. None exists today (`rg` finds no `ColorInput`, `ListDetail` or zone picker in `packages/ui`); `controlFor()` answers a hex `@regex` column and a `@zone` column `{ control: 'input', task: 'text' }` (`sierra/src/resource/field-rules.js` `case 'string'`), so a person types `#3b82f6` or `America/Chicago` by hand. `FJS-D804` and `FJS-D805` already ruled twice that a value native inputs or an existing component can carry is not a new noun; `ui-kit-gaps.md` § Maybe lists Color Picker as data-only (Invariant 13). The red test is not writable until the shape is: every assertion names a component or control that this ruling coins.
  - **A** — Three treatments, no new noun. (1) Colour: `Input` accepts `type="color"` and `Field` wraps it, so label, hint and error come free; `controlFor` answers `{ control: 'input', type: 'color' }` for a column that declares a colour (the declaration is the schema's to spell, a follow-up row, never a sniffed `#rrggbb` pattern). (2) List/detail: a `@frontierjs/css` treatment (`split`) that is two panes wide and one pane under a container query, the selected id staying the app's state. (3) Zone: `controlFor` answers `Combobox` over `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')` for a `@zone` column, the list coming from one `toolbelt/datetime` export.
  - **B** — Three components: `ColorInput` (swatch + hex text + presets), `ListDetail` (owns the one-pane-on-a-phone state, a back affordance and the selected id), `TimeZoneSelect` (a searchable list grouped by region, showing the current offset).
  - **C** — Split by evidence: colour is A (two apps, six copies in ela and linear, native `<input type="color">` is the whole control); list/detail and zone stay held until a second app asks, since `ela` is the only sighting for either — `ui-kit-gaps.md` § One app each already rules that shape.
  - **Recommend C** — a native `<input type="color">` brings the picker, the keyboard and the form value, so it is `FJS-D805`'s shape, not a noun; the other two have one app each, and a phone-collapsing layout needs the app's selection state that a component would only re-declare.

- ~~**FJS-D1240 — Is a `_module.md` with front matter and no body a pass-through for the pages beneath it, and is one with a body and no `<slot />` refused at build?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-18 (`FJS-D1240`): A — split by body: a body-less `_module.md` is a pass-through, its front matter cascading to the pages beneath, which render unwrapped; a `_module.md` with a body and no `<slot />` is refused at build by name.** Found by `FJS-3255`. Luminous Heart's `content/routes/thank-you/_module.md` is `---\nrobots: noindex,nofollow\n---` and nothing else, and `dist/thank-you/index.html` ships `<main class="pages thank-you_index pages-thank-you"></main>`; the legacy Routify site rendered the page. Precedent: SvelteKit, Next, Nuxt, Astro and React Router document no behavior for a layout with no outlet and render nothing; Hugo's `_index.md` `cascade` and Eleventy's directory data files are a data-only file whose front matter reaches descendants; no tool refuses the case by name.
  - **A** — split by body: a body-less `_module.md` is a pass-through, its front matter cascading to the pages beneath, which render unwrapped; a `_module.md` with a body and no `<slot />` is refused at build by name.
  - **B** — refuse every `_module.md` with no `<slot />`, body or not; `ksite port` writes the slot.
  - **C** — pass-through for a body-less one only; a body with no slot keeps rendering empty.
  - **Recommend A** — the file with no body was written as data for its directory, which is what the legacy site did with it, and the one with a body and no slot is the silent empty `<main>` the row reports, so it is refused rather than guessed at.

- ~~**FJS-D1243 — What declares the columns basecamp's `narrowPatch` lists drop, so the lists can go (`FJS-1393`)?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-21 (`FJS-D1243`): A — `@immutable` for set-once, `@system` for bookkeeping, and litestone's update schema emits `readOnly` (`x-litestone-kind: 'transition'`) for the field a `@@transitions` names, since a caller moves it by a MOVE and never by naming it. `id`/`workspaceId`/`createdAt`/`updatedAt`/`deletedAt` are emitted read-only for every model that has them. `narrowPatch(data)` then reads the readOnly set and takes no list.** `FJS-D494` settled the `restated-fields` rule; the fix it blocks cannot start, because the update JSON schema marks none of the ~45 listed columns read-only. Measured by generating the update schema for `db/schema.lite`: `Server.slug`/`status`, `Secret.kind`/`createdBy`/`providerKind`, `Recipe.slug`/`runCount`/`lastRunAt`, `Domain.appId`/`hostname`/`cert*`, `Workspace.accountId`/`ownerId`, `ApiKey.scopes`/`expiresAt`/`lastUsedAt`/`totalUses` and the rest are all `rw`; `id` and `workspaceId` are `rw` on `Recipe` too, and `createdAt`/`updatedAt`/`deletedAt` are absent from it. Only `ApiKey.credentialId`/`tokenHint` (`@system`) are read-only. So `narrowPatch(data)` reading the readOnly set would let every one of them through. Three kinds of column sit in the lists and they want different answers: **set once** (`slug`, `kind`, `provider`, `appId`, `hostname`, `accountId`, `scopes`) · **the app's bookkeeping** (`runCount`, `lastRunAt`, `lastTestAt`, `lastDeliveryAt`, `cert*`, `lastUsedAt`, `totalUses`) · **a `@@transitions` column** (`Server.status`, which the CRUD door must not move behind drain/reboot and which is written only by the machine). `@immutable` fits the first, `@system` the second (and every internal writer then states `system: [...]`, as the 44 existing sites do); the third has no marker — `status` is rw in the update schema, and a `@system`/`@immutable` on it would break the machine's own writes.
  - **A** — `@immutable` for set-once, `@system` for bookkeeping, and litestone's update schema emits `readOnly` (`x-litestone-kind: 'transition'`) for the field a `@@transitions` names, since a caller moves it by a MOVE and never by naming it. `id`/`workspaceId`/`createdAt`/`updatedAt`/`deletedAt` are emitted read-only for every model that has them. `narrowPatch(data)` then reads the readOnly set and takes no list.
  - **B** — as A, but a transitions column stays writable in the update schema (the boundary already refuses an illegal arc) and `Server.status` alone is dropped by a one-name rule in `narrowPatch`; leaves one hand-typed name, which `FJS-D494` says nothing silences.
  - **C** — `narrowPatch` reads the readOnly set only for what the schema already marks, and the rest of each list moves to a per-service `deny` declared in the service's definition next to `model:`, read by `autoValidate`; the schema stays silent on them, so the second statement of the policy survives in a different file.
  - **Recommend A** — it is the only one that leaves no restated list, which is what `FJS-D494` assumed; the transition emit is a small litestone change with `@immutable`/`@sequence` as precedent in the same block of `jsonschema.js`.

- ~~**FJS-D1261 — How does an integrity rule about the STORED row (archived is immutable, a built-in row cannot be edited or deleted, a revoked row is frozen) hold under `asSystem()`?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1261`): A — let `@@require` take `update` and `delete`, graded against the STORED row (the WHERE the write already carries) and kept under `asSystem()`, written as the allowed case (`@@require(update, status != 'archived')`). One word for the data tier; the cost is a second grader shape beside the landing-row one, and a bulk `updateMany` must filter rather than refuse.** `@@require` grades the row as it lands and refuses `update` and `delete` by name (`parser.js` `REQUIRE_OPS`), so the four rules `FJS-3284` could not move — `packages/litestone/example/schema.lite:179` `@@deny(update, status == 'archived')`, maidtech 237/238 `builtIn == true`, maidtech 1367 `revokedAt != null` — stay `@@deny`, and `asSystem()` drops each one: a job or automation edits an archived row.
  - **A** — let `@@require` take `update` and `delete`, graded against the STORED row (the WHERE the write already carries) and kept under `asSystem()`, written as the allowed case (`@@require(update, status != 'archived')`). One word for the data tier; the cost is a second grader shape beside the landing-row one, and a bulk `updateMany` must filter rather than refuse.
  - **B** — a `@@frozen(when: predicate)` model word: once the predicate holds on the stored row, no update or delete lands, for any writer. Says the intent in its name and stays off `@@require`'s landing-row grammar; a fourth integrity word beside `@@check`, `@@require` and `@immutable`.
  - **C** — state it and change nothing: immutability of a stored row is a caller-tier rule (`@@deny`), and a system writer is trusted to honor it; narrow the `access-control.md` line that says `@@require` is "for every writer" to name the landing-row cut.
  - **Recommend A** — `FJS-D1045`'s reason (the word names its tier) already holds for `@@require`, and the stored-row case is the same rule on the other half of the write; B adds a word for what A spells with the one already learned. Confidence 2: no sibling decides the stored-row grader, and `updateMany` is the part A has not priced.

- **FJS-D1262 — Where does the fact "this write reaches these prerendered pages" live?** Found fixing `FJS-3034`. `createReadRecorder` (`packages/sierra/src/build/static-safety.js:276`) taps `db.$tapQuery` per route for the gate check and keeps `models` per ROUTE, then `stop()`s it; nothing keeps `{ model, where, select, orderBy }` per emitted URL. Ghost Q1 measured the per-URL form against a 1,000-post site (publish one draft: predicts 52/52 changed files; edit one body: 1/1, no extra, no miss, 1–2 s) where a full build is 7–15 s and rewrites 939 files byte-identical. The red assertion once built: `affectedPages(manifest, { model: 'Post', op: 'update', before, after })` names the post page, its tag pages, the index pages and the feed — today no function can.
  - **A** — the build derives it: `prerender.js` keeps each URL's reads in a build manifest beside the output, and sierra exports one matcher `affectedPages(manifest, write)` (key match on id/slug/`id in`, else column overlap with select/where/orderBy; an unkeyed filtered read is touched whole; the sitemap is touched whenever a `getStaticPaths()` read is). Nothing is declared, so it cannot drift from the page; the cost is a new build artifact, and the incremental prerender that CONSUMES it (`--only <urls>`) is a second step this ruling does not take.
  - **B** — each route lists `feeds: [Post, Tag]` (the inverse of `publishes:`). Model-level only, so a body edit rebuilds every index page it does not touch, and the list is a restatement that drifts the day a `load()` reads a new model; the build can still check it against the tap and fail on a missing model.
  - **C** — state it and change nothing: a write that must rebuild a page is a job the app writes by hand (`app.jobs`), and `sierra/CLAUDE.md` says so next to `publishes:`. Smallest; leaves the measured 5% of work paid as 100%.
  - **Recommend A** — the tap is already installed and discarded per route, so the manifest is deriving what B would restate by hand (§ V.5), and ghost measured it exact; ship the manifest and matcher only. Confidence 2: the manifest's size on a 100k-page site and whether it ships in `dist/` or beside it are unpriced.

- **FJS-D1263 — Does an update naming only columns the app lists in `system: [...]` need the model's `update` capability?** Found fixing `FJS-3123`. `CapabilityPlugin._checkUpdate` (`packages/litestone/src/plugins/capability.js:110`) charges `update` when `!graded.length && !moves.length`, so a commitment hook's `db.attempt.update({ where, data: { expiredAt }, system: ['expiredAt'] })` is refused for the app principal, which holds no grants; `test/capabilities.test.ts` "a stamp alone is still an update the caller must be granted" (`FJS-1516`) pins that refusal for a principal holding only a move grant. The red assertion: that call as a principal with no capabilities resolves today with `AccessDeniedError … "Attempt.update"`.
  - **A** — a payload whose every key is named in `system: [...]` is charged to no grant (only a bare touch with NO keys is an ordinary update). One rule: `system:` means the application writes it. The `FJS-1516` stamp-alone test flips; the cost is that a service method which names a stamp for a caller holding any or no grant writes it, so the gate on who may reach that method is the service's to hold.
  - **B** — keep the charge and let the commitment fire run holding the grant its model's `@@commitment` implies (the fire's principal carries `<Model>.update` itself), so moodle's workaround becomes the framework's. Leaves `FJS-1516` standing; adds a synthesized grant a person gave nobody, which is the thing the row objects to.
  - **C** — charge a stamp-only write to the model's move grants (`Attempt.expire`, any move the model declares) rather than `update`, so the commitment's own move grant covers its stamp. Needs the fire principal to hold a move grant, which `@system` moves do not require, so it fails for this very case.
  - **Recommend A** — the `system:` list is already the application's statement that the caller is not the editor (`FJS-1516`'s own reason), and the client refuses an entry that is not a `@system`/`@guarded` column or move field, so the exemption cannot reach an ordinary column. Confidence 2: `FJS-1516` chose the opposite for the stamp-alone case with no recorded reason beyond "no finer grant stands in".

- **FJS-D1264 — What mints a mirrored model's key after `litestone mirror exit`?** Found fixing `FJS-3170`. The exit writes `externalId String @unique` with no default (`packages/litestone/src/mirror-exit.js`), so `db.post.create({ data: { message: 'x' } })` on the exited shape is refused `Validation failed — externalId: externalId is required`: the app lists every kept row and creates none. The Provider minted the key; every relation joins on it, so an `@id` cannot stand in. The repo's own schemas split `@default(cuid())` (327) and `@default(uuid())` (280) with `ulid`/`nanoid` rare, so no house default settles the generator.
  - **A** — the exit writes `@default(uuid())` on a String key, says so in its output, and refuses an Int key by name (an Int key has no generator; the author states what mints it). `modeling.md` § uuid-or-Int already names a key that crosses a boundary as uuid's case, which a Provider key is. No new option.
  - **B** — `litestone mirror exit --key-default <uuid|cuid|ulid|nanoid>`, required when a mirrored key is a String: the exit refuses until a generator is named, so nothing is chosen silently. One more flag to restate per run, for a command run once per app.
  - **C** — write nothing and have the exit print that the key needs a minter: the app adds `@default(...)` or a `beforeCreate` hook itself. Smallest; leaves every exited app to hit `externalId is required` first, which is today's defect with a note.
  - **Recommend A** — the exit already writes what the data needs and says what it dropped; a default on the key is the same kind of thing, and the author can change one word afterward. B asks a question whose answer nobody has a reason to vary.
- **FJS-D1265 — What is the one alias grammar for `fli` commands, and how is a namespace's default spelled?** Found fixing `FJS-2983`: 199 command files carry an `alias:`, in four styles — hyphen (`db-migrate`, `site-build`, `cap-setup`), a second namespace (`ws:atlas`, `cf:dns`, `gh:clone`), a prefix-plus-abbreviation (`mksvc`, `mkcmd`, `ecopy`, `dlogs`) and a bare abbreviation (`gs`, `gpl`, `ni`, `nr`, `pub`) — and a namespace's default is `index.md` with `alias: deploy` (title `deploy:all`), `tutor/index.md` with `alias: tutor`, and `project/new.md` with `alias: new`, so `deploy:all`, `tutor:index` and `project:new` are three spellings of one idea. Every option is a rename across `packages/cli/commands/` with every caller moved in the same change (Evolution policy), and `FJS-2982` (taxonomy) moves the same files, so the grammar must be ruled before either row is cut.
  - **A** — One grammar: the alias is the full `ns:verb` and nothing else. `ws:atlas` is the pattern, so `db-migrate`→`db:migrate`, `mksvc`→`make:service`, `gs`→`git:status`; a bare word is allowed only where the command IS a namespace's default (`fli deploy`, `fli tutor`, `fli new`), spelled by one rule: the default is the namespace's `index.md`, titled by the bare namespace (`deploy`, not `deploy:all`). Abbreviations (`gs`, `ni`, `mkc`) are not aliases; a person who wants one writes a `fli make:shortcut` in their own workspace.
  - **B** — Two tiers, both stated. The canonical name is `ns:verb` (the path, nothing declared); the alias is the one SHORT form a person types, and it has one shape — a bare word where the verb is unambiguous (`prove`, `done`, `studio`) and `<ns-initial><verb-initial>` nowhere. The hyphen and `mk`-prefix styles are deleted; namespaced second names (`ws:atlas`, `cf:dns`) are deleted as duplicates of the path. Namespace default: the `index.md`, titled by the bare namespace.
  - **C** — Keep the abbreviations that already earn their place (`gs`, `gp`, `gpl`, `ni`, `nr`, `pub`), since a git-shaped muscle memory is the one thing the whole ecosystem shares, and make everything else A. The default-spelling rule is A's.
  - **Recommend A** — one name for one thing is the Evolution policy's first sentence, and `ns:verb` is already the path, so the alias grammar becomes "there are none"; `fli completion` then lists one spelling per command and `fli list` is the whole surface. B and C keep a second spelling that every doc must teach, which is the failure the row records. A is a mechanical rename (the registry derives the name from the path) and its callers are greppable (`rg 'fli (db-|mk|ws:)'`).

- **FJS-D1266 — How does jetty's `mount()` tell a compiled Mesa component from a function stub?** Found fixing `FJS-3294`. `mount.js:72` sends any function of `length === 2` to Mesa's `mount(label, fn, { props })`, which calls it as `fn(anchor, props)` with a comment node. The shipped fixture `packages/jetty/test/fixtures/basic-ext/src/islands/demo.js` writes `app(root, props)` — a stub — and with `@frontierjs/mesa/runtime` installed it is handed the anchor, not the root, so `root.appendChild` hits a comment node. Red test: `mount(root, (r, p) => { r.textContent = p.x }, { x: 'hi' })` leaves `root.textContent === 'hi'` today only without Mesa installed; with it, the stub never sees `root`. Compiled output is `export default function Name(__anchor, __props)` (`packages/mesa/src/compiler.js` ~10738), re-wrapped as `__mesaHMRWrap` in dev (`mesa-vite/hmr.js:99`).
  - **A** — The compiler stamps the export: a property on the default export (e.g. `Name.$$mesa = true`, or `Symbol.for('mesa.component')`), emitted beside the function and carried by the HMR wrapper; `mount()` keys on it and treats every other function as a stub. Ends the guess for any arity, and a third-party wrapper that re-exports a component keeps it. Costs a line in every compiled file (bench byte gate, `corpus --diff`, `vite-hmr` pins) and a mesa change for a jetty bug.
  - **B** — No guess is needed because the config says: `defineIsland`/`defineDock` take `app` (a compiled component, always Mesa-mounted) and a stub is `{ render(root, props) }` only — the function-stub form is dropped. Change is in jetty alone (`mount.js`, `demo.js`, the `app: (root) => …` tests); the `{ render }` branch of `mountStub` already exists. Costs the one-liner `app: (root) => {…}` form in tests and for extensions that mount no Mesa.
  - **C** — Keep the arity guess and document it: a stub takes `(root)` only. This is what `mount.js` states now, and it fails silently for `(root, props)`.
  - **Recommend B** — jetty is the only caller that must tell the two apart and it already owns the `{ render }` stub shape, so the guess goes without adding a mark to every component in the workspace; A is right if a second consumer (sierra's island mount, a devtools panel) needs to ask "is this a component" of an arbitrary function.

- **FJS-D1267 — How does the browser learn a custom method's gate, so `resource.can('finish')` has something to read?** Found fixing `FJS-2022`. `canAtLevel(gate, op, level)` reads `x-gate`, which litestone emits from `db/schema.lite` (`field-rules.js` `buildGate`, four keys: read/create/update/delete); a `methods:` entry's `gate:` lives in a service file and reaches only `service.describe().methodGates` (`service.ts:2918`), the manifest and mcp — no channel to the bundle. An undeclared method's grade is the model's READ gate (the FJS-826 floor, `customMethodGrade` in `core/litestone.ts`), so only a declared `gate:` is missing. Red test: `createResource('orders').can('finish', 1)` with a service declaring `finish: { gate: 5 }` answers `true` today (unknown name → `GATE_OP[op] ?? op` → no number → permissive).
  - **A** — Sierra's build reads the app's service descriptions (the way `mcp-build.js` already reads the app) and emits `x-method-gates` into the registered schema beside `x-gate`; `buildGate` folds it in, so `can('finish', level)` is the same `canAtLevel` call and an undeclared method falls to `read`. Static; one owner for the grade (`customMethodGrade`); costs a build-time boot of services.
  - **B** — The client asks at runtime: a built-in read call on every service (like `declaredFields`) answers `describe().methodGates` graded for the caller's standing, and `resource.can(name)` awaits/caches it. Graded per standing, no build dependency; costs a round-trip and an async `can()`.
  - **C** — Put the level in the schema: a custom method's gate is declared on the model (`@@gate(... , finish: 5)` or a `@@methods` block), so the existing `x-gate` carries it and junction reads it from there. One source for UI and API; reverses where `methods: [{ gate }]` is declared today.
  - **Recommend A** — `can()` stays synchronous and Invariant 6 holds (affordance only, server enforces); it reuses `customMethodGrade` as the owner. B is the fallback if a build cannot boot services.

- **FJS-D1268 — How does the build import a resource that declares `offlineQuery` so its warm exists before anyone opens the screen — every resource, a named list, or exactly the ones whose source states `offlineQuery`?** Found fixing `FJS-1178`. `declareOffline` runs inside `createResource` at module evaluation (`offline.js:100`, `resource.js:3178`), routes are code-split, so an unopened screen declares nothing; `example/web/src/main.js` imports two resources by hand. Whatever is imported must follow `initJunction` (the `virtual:sierra` client) and lands in the entry chunk that `FJS-D302` grades. Red test: a sierra config with `offline: true` and a resource under `src/resources/` stating `offlineQuery`; the generated `virtual:sierra` text carries no import of it today.
  - **A** — `offline: { resources: true }` as the row sketches: virtual-sierra globs `src/resources/*.mesa` and imports them all after the client is built. Cheapest to write; every resource, offline-relevant or not, lands in the entry chunk and `offline-baseline.json` ratchets up with the app's whole data layer.
  - **B** — `offline: { resources: ['InventoryMovement', 'ProductVariant'] }`: a named list, validated against `src/resources/` filenames (Invariant 19 makes the name the file). Keeps the bundling decision with the app as `main.js` does today, at the cost of a second place that names the same screens the `offlineQuery` already marks.
  - **C** — No new option: the build imports exactly the resources whose source states `offlineQuery` (a scan of `src/resources/`), after the client. The declaration is the one place that says the screen must work with no server, so the entry chunk holds precisely what asked and `offline: true` is enough; an app wanting a resource out of the chunk drops its `offlineQuery`. The scan is a regex over source (an `offlineQuery` built dynamically is missed), and it needs a refusal for a resource whose `createResource` call is not at top level.
  - **Recommend C** — it derives the list from the declaration instead of restating it (§ V.5), adds no config key, and the entry-chunk cost is exactly what the app already opted into; `FJS-D302`'s baseline still grades it.

- **FJS-D1269 — Where does a claimed mirror's refusal lose the Provider's name — conduit's wrapper, junction's forward, or the connector's own text?** Found fixing `FJS-3164`. Rule 2 of `IDEAS/mirror-apps.md` says a surface never learns a Provider noun, but a refused `Post` create reached the room as `mattermost Post.create: mattermost: channelExternalId is not a Mattermost id`. Two pieces are in this tree: `packages/conduit/src/mirror.ts` `mirrored()` prefixes every message with `${provider} ${model}.${verb}:` (and `${provider} answered …`), and junction's `providerOf(m)` builds the `BadGateway` text in `packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts`. The third, the connector's own `need()` text, is in a repo this tree does not hold, and no wrapper can strip a noun out of free text. The red test would assert that a claim whose builder throws `mattermost: x is bad` answers a 400 whose message holds no `mattermost`.
  - **A** — Conduit's wrapper owns it: the surface-visible message is the model and verb only (`Post.create refused: <reason>`), the Provider-named full text goes to `$.log` and the error's `cause`. The connector's own text still leaks, so the claim contract states that a builder's throw is a surface message and names no vendor.
  - **B** — Junction's forward owns it: it catches whatever `forward.*` throws, keeps the status, answers `Post create was refused` plus the caller-input reason only when the error carries a `public` flag, and logs the rest. One owner for every connector, no connector contract.
  - **C** — Rule 2 stays about services and resources, not messages: a refusal naming the vendor is a diagnostic, not a noun the surface learns. `FJS-3164` closes as ruled and rule 2's `rg` note says so.
  - **Recommend B** — Invariant 4 puts the throw-to-status translation in one place, and a forward is where every connector's text arrives; A leaves the leak to each connector's discipline, which is the hand-kept copy this row came from.

- **FJS-D1270 — How does a fli command name the environment it acts on, and does flag value syntax need one spelling?** Found fixing `FJS-2984`. 25 command files document `--production`, `--stage` or `--test` as booleans (`deploy`, `ssh`, `make:enc`, `dev:api`, `fli gui`; `deploy/_module.md:81` ranks `--production > --stage > branch`), while `--env` on `keygen` means *append to this .env file* and names no environment. The `--as` half of the row is not a split: `--as=report` is a layout (`FJS-D401`) and `--as alex@…` on `call`, `db:repl` and `db:export` is a person; the parser takes `--flag value` and `--flag=value` alike, so only the documented examples vary.
  - **A** — One flag `--env <production|stage|test>` on every command that acts on an environment; `keygen`'s output flag becomes `--to` (or `--dotenv`), the three booleans go, and `--test` stops being a mode flag beside `--as`. Predictable, but a rename across 25 command files and `release-view.js:227`.
