  - **B** — As A, but `VITE_API_URL` stays as the one name Vite exposes to a bundle, set from `FRONTIER_API_URL` by Sierra, and `envPrefix` stays `VITE_`. One extra name, and nothing under `FRONTIER_` can reach a browser.
  - **C** — As B, but junction keeps reading bare `PORT` as the deploy's word beside `FRONTIER_PORT_BE`. Fewer deploy files change; `PORT` keeps shadowing the broker's slot, the shadowing the row names.
  - **Recommend B** — the prefix rule holds for every name a person sets, and the browser boundary stays a single name Sierra writes rather than a prefix match. Do the sweep as `IDEAS/lockdown.md` 5.3 says, after 3.3 has junction read the port once, so one pass moves each caller.

- **FJS-D1305 — When a claimed mirror's create lands a row the caller's read policy cannot yet see, what does it answer?** Found fixing `FJS-3148`. `forwardCreate` (`packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts:1283`) lands the Provider's row as the system, then `landMirrored` re-reads it through the caller's client (`getTable(ctx).findUnique`, 1257); for a `Channel` whose read policy is `type == 'open' || members.some(...)` the creator's `ChannelMember` has not landed, so the re-read is `null`, the bridge answers 204 and junction warns *announced something that is not a Channel row*. Red test's assertion: `POST /channels` as alice with `type: 'private'` answers 201/200 with a body naming the new key, today 204 with none.
  - **A** — when the re-read is `null` the create answers 202 with `{ [key]: value }` only: the write happened, the row is not yet readable, and the client re-reads once the sync lands. Nothing is returned that the policy withheld (Invariant 6), and no Provider contract changes; the announce is suppressed for a row the caller cannot read.
  - **B** — the claim's `forward.create` may also return the side rows the Provider made with the row (`{ row, with: { channelMember: [...] } }`), and `landMirrored` lands them as the system before the re-read, so the policy turns true. The answer stays a row, but every Provider whose read policy runs through a relation must return its side rows, and a Provider that cannot has no answer.
  - **C** — keep the 204 and document it as the hazard: a create under a relation-derived read policy answers no row until the sync lands. Cheapest, but the warning stays and `resource.save` gets no row back.
  - **Recommend A** — it is the only one that needs nothing from every Provider and keeps the read policy at the Data boundary; B is the better UX where a Provider can supply side rows and can follow on top of A. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1306 — Do `app.own` and the three `register*` methods share one shape, and if not, which of them is the outlier?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1306`): A — `own` stays; the `register*` family stays and is one shape, `(name, value)`: `registerDevService(name, { url, note })`, the name taken from the key. Callers moved in the same change: `example/api/index.ts` (4), `packages/junction/test/dev-services.test.ts`, `README.md` § dev services, `FJS-D460`'s text. Two of three siblings already decided this shape.** Found fixing `FJS-2963`. `app.own(name, value)` takes one `app.<thing>` slot and throws on a second owner (Invariant 5); `registerMetricsSource(name, fn)` and `registerReadiness(name, fn)` add a keyed entry to a registry; `registerDevService({ name, url, note })` does the same with the name inside the object (`packages/junction/src/core/app.ts:1591`, `FJS-D460`). Nine-question read: *owner* — each store has one writer already (`_metricsSources`, `_readiness`, `_devServices`), so no second implementation exists; *predictability* — the one real mismatch is the argument shape, since two take `(name, value)` and one takes an object carrying its own name; *concept* — `own` (one slot, refuses a second) and `register` (many entries, replaces by name) differ in cardinality and in what a repeat does, so folding them is a noun saved at the price of hiding that. Adjudication: *coherence vs. convention*. Tier: Register. Silence (9th): a repeated `registerDevService` name replaces without a word; that and the shape are `none` — unenforced beyond `test/dev-services.test.ts`. Red test: none yet, the choice decides what it asserts.
  - **A** — `own` stays; the `register*` family stays and is one shape, `(name, value)`: `registerDevService(name, { url, note })`, the name taken from the key. Callers moved in the same change: `example/api/index.ts` (4), `packages/junction/test/dev-services.test.ts`, `README.md` § dev services, `FJS-D460`'s text. Two of three siblings already decided this shape.
  - **B** — one verb for the registries, `app.register('metrics' | 'readiness' | 'devService', name, value)`. One method on `App`, but a string-keyed kind that must be typed by augmentation, and about 30 files change across junction, caravan, conduit, basecamp and the example — every plugin that contributes a metric or a check.
  - **C** — nothing changes; the row is wrong. `own` and `register*` are different ideas (a slot versus a registry entry) and `DevService` is an object because `/manifest` emits it whole with its name. Moves no callers and adds no convention; the cost is the argument-shape mismatch staying.
  - **Recommend A** — the family already decided `(name, value)` twice, and one outlier of three is moved by editing four call sites; C is the reopen path if the owner reads the object shape as a deliberate record. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1307 — What is the one name and the one id key of the principal on both contexts?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1307`): B — `ctx.user` on both contexts (a service gets `ctx.user` where it reads `ctx.auth.user` now), key `id`. Keeps `FJS-D392`'s ruling and the route-side sites; only the service-side sites and the id key move. The word stays *user* for a caller that may not be one.** Found fixing `FJS-2955`. A route reads `ctx.user` (flat, nullable), a service reads `ctx.auth.user` (frozen, propagates) — `packages/junction/CLAUDE.md` § TransportContext vs ServiceContext — and auth's session carries `userId` where a hand-built principal carries `id`, so `toDataPrincipal` (`packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts:3673`) copies `userId` onto `id` for Litestone's `auth().id` and `example/api/src/core/channels.ts:64` reads `session?.userId ?? session?.id`. `IDEAS/lockdown.md` 3.11 says `ctx.principal` on both, but `FJS-D392` ruled `ctx.user` stays the principal, and the rename is about 249 sites in 87 files. No red test yet: the fix is a rename, and its assertion (`ctx.principal` on both, no `userId` key) depends on the answer.
  - **A** — `ctx.principal` on both contexts, key `id` only. `ctx.user` and `ctx.auth.user` are deleted (`ctx.auth` keeps what is not the person); auth's session shape moves `userId` to `id` and `toDataPrincipal`'s copy is deleted; `SessionContext`, `services.ts` and the 249 sites move in one change. *Principal* is the ARCHITECT word and stays true when the caller is a Machine or a Peer (`FJS-D1198`), where *user* does not.
  - **B** — `ctx.user` on both contexts (a service gets `ctx.user` where it reads `ctx.auth.user` now), key `id`. Keeps `FJS-D392`'s ruling and the route-side sites; only the service-side sites and the id key move. The word stays *user* for a caller that may not be one.
  - **C** — keep both homes, unify only the id key to `id`. Smallest change and it closes the `userId ?? id` read; the row's first complaint (two homes) stays, with the table in `CLAUDE.md` as its documentation.
  - **Recommend B** — it matches the two siblings from this question's own domain: `FJS-D392` kept `ctx.user` as the principal, and `FJS-D1196`/`FJS-D1198` ruled a machine or peer is a User (`User.kind`), which removes A's only argument for a new word; it also moves the service-side sites and the id key rather than all 249. A is the reopen path if the owner wants lockdown 3.11's `ctx.principal` spelling. Confidence 2: whether auth's session row `id` (the session itself) collides with a principal `id` is unchecked and decides how wide the auth edit is. Nine questions answered after the filing, not before.

- **FJS-D1308 — How does a signed inbound delivery past the 256 KB body bound, or a non-UTF-8 or multipart one, reach a verifier as the bytes the sender signed?** Found fixing `FJS-2927`. Measured through `createApp().post('/hook')`: a 300 KB JSON post is a 413 before any handler runs, and no per-route bound exists (`RouteOptions` is `body: 'stream'` and `middleware` only, so the only lever is the app-wide `http.maxBodySize`); a multipart post has `ctx.rawBody === undefined`; a text post of invalid UTF-8 has a `rawBody` whose replacement characters are not the signed bytes. The bytes themselves are already kept — `await ctx.$raw.$req.arrayBuffer()` returns all of them on a parsed route, multipart included — so the gap is the bound and `conduit`'s `Delivery.rawBody: string` (`packages/conduit/src/receive.ts`), which every dialect verifies and which `plugin.ts` fills with `ctx.rawBody ?? ''`. The red test: a receiver route given a 300 KB signed body answers 413 today, and 200 once it verifies.
  - **A** — `RouteOptions.maxBodySize` (per route, falling back to `http.maxBodySize`) read by the one `parseBody` call; conduit's receiver route states its own bound (a receiver field, default the app's); `Delivery.rawBody` becomes the bytes (`Uint8Array`), filled from `$raw.$req`, and a dialect that needs text decodes it. One owner for the bound and one spelling for the signed body.
  - **B** — no junction option: the receiver route is `{ body: 'stream' }`, and conduit reads the stream to its own cap and parses JSON/urlencoded itself. Junction is untouched, but conduit grows a second body parser beside `transport/body.ts`.
  - **C** — no code: an app that takes vendor deliveries raises `http.maxBodySize` for the whole app, and `Delivery` reads bytes from `$raw.$req`. Every route then accepts the larger body, which is the exposure the 256 KB default is for.
  - **Recommend A** — the bound is a property of the route that verifies a signature, not of the app, and `parseBody` is already the one place a bound is enforced (Invariant 4); B duplicates the parser and C widens every route. Confidence 2: no sibling decided a per-route bound, so this is the no-precedent case and A adds one option; C is the reopen path if the owner wants none.

- ~~**FJS-D1309 — Is an `Idempotency-Key` replay graded against the caller's standing at replay, or is the stored answer final?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1309`): B — the stored answer is final: `FJS-D451` gains the sentence that the skipped gate is the price, bounded by bytes already sent and by `ttl`, and that revocation is a session or key matter, which already ends replay. No code; the ruling's ninth question gains the test that a revoked session's replay is refused.** Found framing `FJS-1354`. `FJS-D451` rules that a replay runs nothing, and the gate is part of what it skips: `callService` claims the key after the method selection and the scopes and before the pipeline (`packages/junction/src/core/service.ts:686`), and a replay sets `ctx.result` and returns, so `gateAuth` never runs. A revoked SESSION or key cannot replay, since a key needs a principal (`idempotency.ts` `principalOf`) and the principal is verified before the call; what replays is a caller still signed in whose standing dropped, for up to `idempotency.ttl` (24 hours). Bounded: the answer is bytes that caller was already sent. Prior art is silent on authorization at replay: Stripe stores the first status and body and compares parameters ([docs.stripe.com/idempotency](https://docs.stripe.com/idempotency)), and the IETF draft asks only for a composite key with "client specific attributes" ([draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header-07](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header) § 5). The red test under A: a caller writes with a key, their level drops below the verb's gate, the same request replays — 403 expected, the stored 201 today.
  - **A** — re-grade at replay: before `ctx.result = idem.result`, `callService` asks the grader `gateAuth` asks, `callerGateLevel(db, accessor, session)` (`core/litestone.ts:3392`), against the verb's level (a CRUD verb's `@@gate`, a custom method's `customMethodGrade`), and throws the gate's own refusal when the caller no longer reaches it. No hook, no method, no row read; a row policy is not re-asked, since a replay reads no row. `FJS-D451` is amended to "runs nothing but the level check".
  - **B** — the stored answer is final: `FJS-D451` gains the sentence that the skipped gate is the price, bounded by bytes already sent and by `ttl`, and that revocation is a session or key matter, which already ends replay. No code; the ruling's ninth question gains the test that a revoked session's replay is refused.
  - **Recommend A** — `FJS-D300` is this domain's sibling (a replayed mutation is graded at the level the caller has AT replay) and its reason holds here: the server re-checks its own reading of the caller regardless (Invariant 6). The cost is one call to a grader that already exists, not a second implementation beside it. Confidence 2: D300's replay runs the write and this one runs nothing, which is B's whole argument.

- ~~**FJS-D1310 — Does `POST /auth/register` answer the same for a taken address as for a free one, and what does it answer?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1310`): A — equalized, verify-first, derived from whether the app can mail: where `onEmailVerificationRequested` is set, register answers 202 `{ ok: true }` for every address, a free one gets a verification mail and its account is created at the click, which signs in — never at the request (the `FJS-D888` shape), and a taken one gets a mail to its owner ("someone tried to register; sign in or reset"); the answer is sent before the lookup, as reset's is. Where no verification mail is configured there is no mailbox to equalize with and the route keeps today's 201/409. The scaffold's register page becomes "check your email". Supabase's line, drawn by the app's own wiring rather than an option.** Found framing `FJS-3197` (ASVS 5.0 V6.3.8, L3). The route is `createUser` + `login` in one step and answers 201 with a session (`packages/auth/plugin.ts:206`); `createUser` throws `EmailTakenError` (409) for a taken address (`auth.ts:1816`). Every other door this package keeps is already equalized, on the clock as well: `login` pays `payPasswordCost` (`FJS-063`), `requestPasswordReset` answers before its lookup (`FJS-1832`), and a magic link creates an unknown address's account at the CLICK, never at the request (`FJS-D888`). Equalizing register means register can no longer sign anyone in, since a session for a free address and none for a taken one is the same oracle. Prior art: OWASP's correct example for account creation is "A link to activate your account has been emailed to the address provided" ([Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html)); Supabase with email confirmation on answers a taken address with an obfuscated user and no error, and with it off answers "User already registered" ([supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/auth-signup](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/v1/auth-signup)) — equalized exactly when a mailbox proves the address.
  - **A** — equalized, verify-first, derived from whether the app can mail: where `onEmailVerificationRequested` is set, register answers 202 `{ ok: true }` for every address, a free one gets its account unverified with a verification mail whose click signs in, and a taken one gets a mail to its owner ("someone tried to register; sign in or reset"); the answer is sent before the lookup, as reset's is. Where no verification mail is configured there is no mailbox to equalize with and the route keeps today's 201/409. The scaffold's register page becomes "check your email". Supabase's line, drawn by the app's own wiring rather than an option.
  - **B** — keep the 409 and rule it accepted: register signs in at once, the oracle is bounded by `registerRateLimit` (5 per 15 minutes, `plugin.ts:50`), and V6.3.8 is an L3 requirement this package records as not met, by ruling, in `docs/ASVS.md`.
  - **C** — an option, `register: { equalize: true }`, B by default and A opt-in. A flag that widens the shoulder (*paved road vs. the workaround*) for a choice the app's mail wiring already states.
  - **Recommend A** — the package's own siblings decided it three times (login, reset, magic link), and the magic link already creates an account at the click; deriving the line from the mail wiring is the Supabase shape with no option added. Confidence 2: it changes every scaffold's sign-up from signed-in to check-your-email, which is a product call B keeps.

- ~~**FJS-D1311 — Who keeps a reset or magic-link token in the URL away from the analytics vendor: the tag, the token page, or the link?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1311`): C — the link: the token rides the fragment, `/reset/#token=`. A fragment is never sent in a request, a `Referer` or a server's access log, and Plausible ignores it unless `hashBasedRouting` is on. The scaffold's two links and `auth`'s documented examples (`types.ts:111`, `:123`, `:360`) move, and the token pages read `location.hash`; whether GTM's built-in page variables carry the fragment is unprobed.** Found framing `FJS-3205`. Sierra hands a custom provider `pageUrl()`, origin + pathname (`packages/sierra/src/analytics/index.js:84`, `FJS-1083`), but the built-in providers load the vendor's auto-tracking tag (`analytics/tag.js:25`), which reads the address bar itself. The scaffold's links are `/reset/?token=` and `/verify-email/?token=` (`packages/cli/commands/project/new.md:1111`), and the token page POSTs the token (`FJS-D616`). Vendor behavior, from the vendors' docs, not probed: Plausible "strips all query parameters" at ingestion by default, so the token reaches its server and is not stored, and its tag offers `autoCapturePageviews: false` with a manual pageview carrying a `url` ([plausible.io/docs/script-extensions](https://plausible.io/docs/script-extensions)); GA4 can redact named query parameters in the browser before collection, configured per web stream in Google's UI, not in the page ([GA4 Data redaction](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/13544947)); a GTM container's tags are the app's and Sierra cannot see them. The red test under any option: a drive loads `/reset/?token=t` with the built-in Plausible tag and asserts no request to the vendor carries `t`.
  - **A** — the tag: Sierra loads Plausible with auto-capture off and sends each pageview itself with `pageUrl()`, the address `FJS-1083` already settled for a custom provider; for GTM, Sierra pushes `page_location` into `dataLayer` without the search. Plausible is closed; GTM only as far as the container's tags read `page_location`, which Sierra cannot grade.
  - **B** — the token page: the scaffold's reset, verify and magic-link pages read the token and strip it with `history.replaceState` before anything else runs. Covers every vendor, but a prerendered page carries the tag in its `<head>` ahead of the page's own script (`vendorTagHtml`), so the strip races the tag there, and every page that takes a token has to remember.
  - **C** — the link: the token rides the fragment, `/reset/#token=`. A fragment is never sent in a request, a `Referer` or a server's access log, and Plausible ignores it unless `hashBasedRouting` is on. The scaffold's two links and `auth`'s documented examples (`types.ts:111`, `:123`, `:360`) move, and the token pages read `location.hash`; whether GTM's built-in page variables carry the fragment is unprobed.
  - **Recommend C** — it removes the token from every reader at once, the vendor's, the `Referer`'s and the access log's, with no race and no vendor setting, by moving the one place the link is written; A is still worth doing for Plausible as defense in depth, and is the reopen path if a mailer that rewrites links drops fragments. Confidence 2: the GTM half is unprobed, and the drive above settles it.

- **FJS-D1312 — Does focus on a text field in the terminal shell set INSERT, as `FJS-D987` rules, or NORMAL, as `Shell.mesa` has done since 2781dd2b?** Found fixing `FJS-2931`. `FJS-D987` (owner, over "vim proper") says a text field is INSERT the moment focus lands, and `example/web/test/verify-tui.mjs:179` asserts it. `Shell.mesa`'s `focusin` sets `normal` on every focus except the jump field, because a route focuses its first control on mount and a scene that opened already typing would eat the h j k l it walks on; no ruling records that change. Red assertion today: after Tab onto the path field the footer reads NORMAL, never INSERT, so `the path field is INSERT the moment focus lands on it` fails.
  - **A** — keep `FJS-D987`: `focusin` sets `insert` on a text field. The drive passes unchanged and no key is spent before typing; the cost is the autofocus case above, so a route whose first control is a text field opens swallowing h j k l until Esc.
  - **B** — amend `FJS-D987` to the code: every focus is NORMAL, `i`, `a` or `/` enters INSERT. The drive's L179 check and the CLAUDE.md line in sierra move with it, and the ruling's "keeps every drive that types into a field working unchanged" is given up; any drive that types straight after Tab must send `i` first.
  - **C** — A, except a field that took focus from a route's own mount-time autofocus (no key or Tab preceded it) stays NORMAL. Both stated cases hold, but the shell has to tell a user's Tab from a mount's `focus()`, a new distinction to keep.
  - **Recommend B** (confidence 2) — the code's reason is a measured failure on the Haven scenes, the one surface with strangers on it, while A's cost (one key before typing) is the vim habit the shell already teaches; C buys back that key with a heuristic about who called `focus()`.

- **FJS-D1313 — How does auth finish an OAuth flow that connects a TENANT's third-party account — tokens to the app's own row, not a Credential and a session?** Found fixing `FJS-2246`. Measured in the tree 2026-10-19: `OauthFlow` (`packages/auth/db/auth.lite:242`) holds `state`, `provider`, `verifier`, `returnTo` and `invitation` and no initiator or tenant; `oauthCallback` (`auth.ts:1472`) returns `{ identity, tokens, returnTo, link }` and the route then runs `oauthResolve`, which finds-or-creates a person and issues a session. `FJS-D960` ruled `connections.connect(provider)` for a signed-in person's second sign-in provider and it is not built (no `connect` in `services.ts`), and a delegated grant — Google Business Profile for an agency, Jobber for an account — wants neither a sign-in nor a `Credential` row: it wants `tokens` written to the app's own row, as the system inside the account the admin chose, for `FJS-D848`'s `getFor` resolver to spend. The red test: begin a flow for a provider declared for the grant, complete the callback, and assert no `User`, `Credential` or session was created and the tokens reached the app's row.
  - **A** — one more flow kind on the existing engine: `oauthBegin` takes `grant: { model, id }` (and `OauthFlow` gains a guarded `grant` and the initiator's id, set only by a service that has graded the caller), and the callback route, seeing a grant on the flow, calls the provider's `onGrant({ tokens, identity, grant, initiator })` that the app declares in `oauthProviders` instead of `oauthResolve`. A provider declares `purpose: 'grant'` and is then absent from `oauthProviderNames()` for sign-in, so `google-business` cannot become a login. Auth owns the redirect, state, PKCE and exchange; the app owns where the tokens land, so auth never learns the app's `Integration` model.
  - **B** — `connections.connect(provider, { into })` completes `FJS-D960` and widens it: the Credential row stays the one place tokens land, with `ownerType`/`ownerId` (user, or the app's row), and conduit's resolver reads `Credential` for any owner. Nothing app-specific to wire, but a rotating refresh token then lives in auth's table rather than the app's `@secret` field, and an app whose row already holds the tokens (Jobber's `Integration`) copies them across.
  - **C** — no auth change: the app composes `oauthBegin`/`oauthCallback` itself in its own routes, as serpgrid did, and auth documents it. Costs each app the pending-row-by-state-digest workaround `FJS-2246` records, and nothing marks the provider as grant-only.
  - **Recommend A** — `oauthBegin`/`oauthCallback` are already the right engine and serpgrid spent them as-is, so what is missing is only the flow's purpose and who it was started for, both facts the flow row can carry; `onGrant` keeps the write at the app's model, where its gate and `@secret` already are, and `purpose` answers the sign-in-provider leak without a second provider table. B moves the tokens out of the row `FJS-D848`'s resolver is already written against, and C is the status quo the row exists to end. Confidence 2: no sibling ruled whether tokens may leave `Credential`.

- **FJS-D1314 — Which side of each cross-package duplicate export gives way, and does `fli ws:exports` grade the rest?** Found fixing `FJS-2966`. Measured 2026-10-19: `session` is `sierra/resource` (the signed-in state object, `src/resource/session.js:63`) and `@frontierjs/testing` (a `SessionContext` builder, `src/index.ts:310`); `track` is `sierra/analytics` (an event, `index.js:138`) and `mesa/runtime` (a reactive-proxy wrap, `runtime.js:5871`); `mount` is `jetty/runtime` (`mount.js:62`, a page into an extension root) and `mesa/runtime` (a component into a renderer, `runtime-terminal.js:2177`, `runtime-native.js:569`); `status` is `sierra/resource` (the connection state, `index.js:131`) and `litestone/migrations` (`migrations.js:760`, applied-vs-pending); `parseQuery` is junction's HTTP query-string parser (`transport/body.ts:404`) and litestone-base's `{limit,where,orderBy}` builder (`core/litestone.ts:278`), already exported as `parseLitestoneQuery`, so one name is spelled two ways. `FJS-D1219` struck `createTestEnv` as one thing at two layers; none of these five is that, since each pair does a different job.
  - **A** — rename the narrower side to its job: testing `session` → `sessionContext`, mesa `track` stays and sierra `track` → `trackEvent`, jetty `mount` → `mountPage`, litestone `status` → `migrationStatus` (sierra's `status` stays), junction's litestone `parseQuery` → `parseListQuery` at the source so the `as` alias goes. Every caller moves in one change. Then `fli ws:exports` fails a name two packages export unless a `DECISIONS.md` ruling lists the pair, as it already fails a subpath spelled twice (`FJS-2964`).
  - **B** — rename only where one import list can hold both (`parseQuery`, and `session`/`track` where a test or sierra file imports both); `mount` and `status` live on different subpaths and a reader never sees them side by side. `fli ws:exports` grades only same-subpath-or-same-barrel clashes.
  - **C** — rename nothing but `parseQuery`, and have `fli ws:exports` print the cross-package duplicates as a note with no failure. Cheapest, and the register row narrows to a report.
  - **Recommend A** (confidence 2) — one name for one thing is the policy and nothing here has shipped, so no caller is stranded; B and C leave the five-line list the row exists to remove, and a grader with a named allowance list is what keeps the sixth collision from arriving.

- ~~**FJS-D1315 — Which one verb set do the client, sierra's session and jetty's resources share for auth, and which one name is the token option?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1315`): A — the client's names win at every layer: sierra `submitCode` → `completeSignIn`, jetty `login`/`submitCode`/`logout` → `signIn`/`completeSignIn`/`signOut`; the token option stays `tokenKey` in the two config files (a string a config can hold) and `tokenStorage` in the client (an object), because they are two different things, one built from the other, and the bridge index names both.** Found fixing `FJS-2969`. Measured 2026-10-19: the client (`junction/src/client/index.ts`) has `signIn`, `signInWithMagicLink`, `completeSignIn(code, challenge?)`, `signInWith(provider)`, `signUp`, `signOut`; sierra's `session.js` re-exports `signIn`, `submitCode`, `signUp`, `signOut` (`submitCode` calls `auth.completeSignIn`, `session.js:267`); jetty's `resources` says `login`, `submitCode`, `logout` (`active-port.js:120-152`). The token option is `tokenKey` (a localStorage key string) in `sierra.config.js`, `jetty.config.js` and the scaffolds, and `tokenStorage` (a `TokenStore` object) in the client, which sierra builds from the key with `localTokenStore(tokenKey)` (`sierra/src/resource/index.js:374-384`). Callers of `submitCode`: basecamp login, example web `_module.mesa`, example extension dock.
  - **A** — the client's names win at every layer: sierra `submitCode` → `completeSignIn`, jetty `login`/`submitCode`/`logout` → `signIn`/`completeSignIn`/`signOut`; the token option stays `tokenKey` in the two config files (a string a config can hold) and `tokenStorage` in the client (an object), because they are two different things, one built from the other, and the bridge index names both.
  - **B** — A, and the config option is renamed `tokenStorage` too, taking either a key string or a `TokenStore`, so one name reaches from `sierra.config.js` to `createJunctionClient`. Costs a dual-typed option.
  - **C** — the sierra/jetty names win (`submitCode`, `login`, `logout`) and the client renames `completeSignIn` → `submitCode`; `signInWith`/`signInWithMagicLink` stay. Shortest for the apps, but a code is not always submitted (a ticket is completed) and the bridge index, `FJS-D915` and the client tests all say `completeSignIn`.
  - **Recommend A** (confidence 2) — one name for one thing and nothing has shipped, so every caller moves in the same change; `tokenKey` and `tokenStorage` name different things, so they are not drift.

- ~~**FJS-D1316 — Is junction's `client.resource(name, idField, opts)` a public door that takes `(name, opts)` like `createResource`, or internal to sierra?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1316`): A — keep it public and give it `createResource`'s shape: `client.resource(name, { idField, match, … })`, no positional argument. A junction app with no sierra keeps its one-call door; the README, the fullstack example and `client-types`/`realtime-events` tests move in the same change.** `FJS-D866` landed the Store half (`Tether`, `TetherBinding`, `useTether`) and `FJS-2933` cut `createResource` to `(name, opts?)`; what is left of `FJS-2967` is a second factory with a positional `idField` (`junction/src/client/index.ts` `resource()`), called by sierra at `resource.js:939` and `:3137`, shown in `junction/README.md:2084` and the fullstack example, and `IDEAS/lockdown.md` 3.7 says only "`client.resource` internal" with no ruling behind it.
  - **A** — keep it public and give it `createResource`'s shape: `client.resource(name, { idField, match, … })`, no positional argument. A junction app with no sierra keeps its one-call door; the README, the fullstack example and `client-types`/`realtime-events` tests move in the same change.
  - **B** — internal: not documented, not in the README, renamed with an underscore or moved behind sierra's import. A standalone junction app builds its own `service()` + `Tether` + `bind`, and the browser has one public Resource noun, sierra's.
  - **C** — leave the positional `idField` and only record that `createResource` is the app-facing noun.
  - **Recommend A** — the positional argument is the real drift (`FJS-2933` already refused it on the other factory), and junction used on its own is a stated case (`FJS-D138`); B removes it to save one name.

- **FJS-D1317 — Does a derived service name keep its filename spelling as a mounted alias (`FJS-D159`), or is the alias removed so one service answers to one name?** Found fixing `FJS-2960`: the `channels:` half of the row is already deleted (`packages/notifications/plugin.ts` has no such refusal), and what remains is `ServiceRegistry.aliasesOf` (`packages/junction/src/core/service.ts:3156`), which `FJS-D159` ruled in on purpose so `product-variants.service.ts` serves `productVariants` AND `/product-variants`. Consumers: `app.ts:2565` (shadow check), `app-model.ts:97` (`aliases`), `example/api/app.snapshot.md` (*also answers to*), `test/service-name-kebab.test.ts`. `FJS-D159` is a ruling, so removal re-rules it; nothing has shipped, so no URL is held by it.
  - **A** — keep the alias; record in `FJS-D159` that it is a ruled mount, not a compat shim, and drop it from `lockdown.md`'s "Done when" list.
  - **B** — remove it: the service is `productVariants` only, the mount is `/productVariants`, and a multi-word file either is camelCase-named or declares `name:`. `aliasesOf`, the `register` aliases argument, the shadow-loop spelling, `app-model` `aliases` and the snapshot line go in one change; the kebab test becomes a refusal test.
  - **C** — invert it: the kebab spelling is the one mounted name (URL and WS frame), the camelCase accessor is only the model/resource key, and no registry alias exists.
  - **Recommend B** — Evolution policy: one name for one thing, and a URL costs an app nothing before release; `FJS-D159`'s only stated reason for the alias was URLs already shipped. Confidence 2: `FJS-D159` also argued a rename moves every multi-word URL, which B accepts, so the owner may still prefer A. Adjudication: *preservation vs. evolution*, tier Register.

- ~~**FJS-D1318 — Which door lists rows on a screen: `resource.list()` alone, or `tether` + `load()` with `list()` as sugar over them?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1318`): A — `list()` is the one door for a screen's rows: `load`, `more`, `stale`, `tether` leave the resource's public keys (they remain what `list()` is built from, and `useTether` stays for non-list reads), the 23 `.load()` pages and the 40 `service.find()` pages that render a list move to `list()` (`{ composed: true }` where `find()` answers an include or count), and a `fli check` rule grades a route calling `.load(` or `.service.find(` to fill a table.** Found fixing `FJS-2968`. Measured 2026-10-19 over `example/` and `packages/basecamp/`: `list()` is read in 5 `.mesa` files, `tether`/`useTether` in 23, `.load()` in 23, `.service.find()` in 40 and `.service.on()` in 16. `example`'s products page runs `useTether(products.tether)` + `products.load()`; basecamp's servers page keeps `rows` by hand from `servers.service.find(page.query, …)` and merges custom-method pushes (`servers heartbeat`, `drain`) in a `service.on('*')` handler; `sierra/README.md` and `list.js` teach `list()`. `IDEAS/lockdown.md` 3.7 (proposed) says `list()` is the one URL-driven list and `store`/`load`/`stale`/`more` become its internals — nothing in `DECISIONS.md` rules it, and a page that wants a count beside the rows or a hand-merged custom-method push has no stated door today. The red check would be a `fli check` rule failing a route that lists through `service.find()` / `.load()`; it cannot be written until the answer exists.
  - **A** — `list()` is the one door for a screen's rows: `load`, `more`, `stale`, `tether` leave the resource's public keys (they remain what `list()` is built from, and `useTether` stays for non-list reads), the 23 `.load()` pages and the 40 `service.find()` pages that render a list move to `list()` (`{ composed: true }` where `find()` answers an include or count), and a `fli check` rule grades a route calling `.load(` or `.service.find(` to fill a table.
  - **B** — two named doors: `list()` for a URL-driven list, `tether` + `load()` for rows a screen holds without the URL (pickers, embedded panels, a dashboard tile); `service.find()` stays for one-off reads that need the envelope. Basecamp's servers page and example's products page both move to `list()`, the README says which door answers which question, and no key leaves the resource.
  - **C** — leave the surface and fix the two pages only: servers moves to `tether` + `load()` (as example does), and the README drops `list()` as the headline. Cheapest, and the one door left is the one that cannot carry `page.query`/`page.directives` without the page restating the five wirings `list.js` says every page restated.
  - **Recommend A** (confidence 2) — one name for one thing and nothing has shipped, so the 23 pages move in one change; B keeps the three-way answer the row exists to remove under better labels. Confidence 2 because servers' hand-merge of custom-method pushes (`heartbeat`, `drain`) has to be proved by a tether-backed list in a browser before the servers page moves, and no drive covers it today.

- **FJS-D1319 — What do sierra's setup verbs mean: does `init*` start something and `configure*` only store, and does analytics keep both or export one?** Found fixing `FJS-2962`. Measured 2026-10-19: seven `init*` (`initJunction`, `initRouter`, `initTheme`, `initToolbar`, `initSession`, `initPrefetch`, `initAnalytics`) and three `configure*` (`configureFetch`, `configureAnalytics`, `configureLocalDb`). By side effect the split is not clean: `configureFetch` stores options and swaps the fetch (no start) and `configureAnalytics` resolves a provider and loads nothing (`FJS-D608`/`FJS-2058` made it the island entry's call because the tag is already in the HTML), but `configureLocalDb` (`packages/sierra/src/resource/local-db.js:55`) registers `pagehide` listeners and opens the OPFS engine at once, which is an `init*`. Red test: `configureLocalDb` is exported from `sierra/resource` and the rule "a `configure*` starts nothing" fails on it.
  - **A** — keep the split by side effect, as `lockdown.md` 3.13 words it: `configure*` stores and starts nothing, `init*` starts something. `configureLocalDb` becomes `initLocalDb` (virtual-sierra, `resource/index.js`, the test, README); analytics keeps both exports because they are different acts (resolve vs. resolve and load), and the rule is written once in `VOCABULARY.md`.
  - **B** — the same rename, and analytics exports one verb: `initAnalytics(config, { load: false })` for the island entry, and `configureAnalytics` goes. One name per module, at the price of an option that switches a side effect off.
  - **C** — one verb for all ten (`init*`), the `configure*` spelling removed; the stores-only distinction lives in each doc comment, not the name.
  - **Recommend A** — the plan's own words are *split by side effect*, and analytics' two acts really are two (`FJS-D608`'s ruling gave the island entry the no-load half); B trades a second name for a boolean that flips the verb's meaning. Confidence 2: the row's *analytics picks one* reads as B, so the owner may mean it.

- ~~**FJS-D1320 — Where does a module live that api and web both import: `db/`, a new root `shared/` directory, or nowhere?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-30 (`FJS-D1320`): D — Both, split by origin. `db/` keeps code the schema points at (an `@computed` body, a merge field catalog) under `#db/*`. A root `shared/` holds every other pure module two surfaces run (haven's dice, checkers and sky clock) under `#shared/*`. Neither imports a surface or junction. `db/` may import `shared/`, but `shared/` imports nothing app-side. A `shared/` file must be pure (no I/O, no side effects) and imported by two or more surfaces, and `app-layout` warns on one that only a single surface imports. `FJS-D627` gains the exemption: an import through `#db` or `#shared` is allowed, and a relative import across surfaces is still refused. `shared/` is a root directory that is not a surface, so Invariant 3 and README § Project Structure say so. Cost: a ninth root name, and two `"imports"` entries in the app `package.json`.** Found fixing `FJS-2740`. `FJS-D627` rules that a file two surfaces run belongs to the side that runs it and the other gets its output through a service, which answers a query or a template but not pure code BOTH sides run: ela's `db/computed.ts` (quote totals, live in the form and authoritative on save) and `db/merge.ts` (the one merge renderer and field catalog) are imported as `../../../db/x.ts` from api and `../../../../db/x.ts` from web, the root tsconfig `include` omits `db/`, and no alias names it, so they are type-checked only when something imports them. README § Project Structure names no folder for them and `fli find` returns no row.
  - **A** — A root `shared/` directory beside the surfaces, aliased `#shared/*` in the app tsconfig and in Sierra's `appAliasPlugin`, `include`d by the scaffolded tsconfig. A new directory, a new noun and an Invariant 3 entry; `app-layout` must learn that it is not a surface, and the D627 warning must exempt an import of it. Cost: a ninth root name for what is usually a handful of files.
  - **B** — `db/` is the sanctioned home for schema-derived pure modules (`computed.ts`, a field catalog): the directory is already "shared by all sub-projects, owned by none", the ela precedent needs no file moved, and the fix is the scaffolded tsconfig `include` of `db/**/*.ts` plus an alias (`#db/*`) in tsconfig and `appAliasPlugin` so the `../../../` ladder goes. Cost: a module with no schema ancestor (a date formatter) has no home and falls to C.
  - **C** — No folder: D627 stands and the answer is a service call or an app-local workspace package (`packages/<x>` in the app's own workspaces) that both surfaces depend on by name. Cost: a quote total shown live in a form then needs a round trip or its own package for a 40-line function.
  - **D** — Both, split by origin. `db/` keeps code the schema points at (an `@computed` body, a merge field catalog) under `#db/*`. A root `shared/` holds every other pure module two surfaces run (haven's dice, checkers and sky clock) under `#shared/*`. Neither imports a surface or junction. `db/` may import `shared/`, but `shared/` imports nothing app-side. A `shared/` file must be pure (no I/O, no side effects) and imported by two or more surfaces, and `app-layout` warns on one that only a single surface imports. `FJS-D627` gains the exemption: an import through `#db` or `#shared` is allowed, and a relative import across surfaces is still refused. `shared/` is a root directory that is not a surface, so Invariant 3 and README § Project Structure say so. Cost: a ninth root name, and two `"imports"` entries in the app `package.json`.
  - **Recommend B** — precedent outranks an invented directory: ela already put schema-derived code in `db/`, `PHILOSOPHY.md` § V's *derived* question says that code traces back to `schema.lite`, and `shared/` (A) is the reopen path if a non-schema module turns up twice. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1321 — Is copying a row a framework verb, where does it live, and how does the schema name a row's owned children — or does each app keep writing it?** Found fixing `FJS-2675`. Two ela instances (a survey template's `duplicate`, a `@@hasTemplates` model's `fromTemplate`) both copy a row minus `@id`, `@unique`/partial-`@@unique` columns, `@default(now())`/`@updatedAt`, tenant and `@system` stamps, plus its children. The schema names every one of those columns, but nothing names which relations are OWNED children (a `SurveyTemplateScore` row belongs to its template; a `User` reference does not), and `@@hasTemplates` knows the template flag but not the `@sequence` to renumber. The red test is a `duplicate` on a model with a `@unique` column: today there is no such method on `db.<model>`, the auto service or a Resource.
  - **A** — Litestone client verb `db.<model>.copy(id, overrides?, { with: ['children'] })`: drops `@id`, `@unique`, the clock columns, tenant and `@system` (Invariant 6, so the gate still grades the insert), and the caller lists the children to carry by relation name. Nothing new in the schema; the app still names the flag to clear and the title suffix in `overrides`. Cost: a unique column with no override is a refusal by name, and each app still writes a 5-line wrapper for ela's `(Copy)` naming.
  - **B** — A schema declaration of ownership first (`@relation(..., owned)` or `@@owns(scores)`, which also gives `@@softDelete(cascade)` and a deep delete the same word), then A's verb copies the owned set by default. Cost: a new attribute and a noun, a parser, a differ and a `jsonschema` change before the verb, and `FJS-D62`-style precedent says to wait for a third caller.
  - **C** — No framework verb: the row stays "each app writes a typed method", and Litestone ships only the helper that answers *which columns a copy must drop* (`db.$copyable(accessor)` beside `$protectedFields`), so the two ela methods stop re-deriving it by hand. Cost: the children and the template flag stay hand-written, which is where the second instance's bugs were.
  - **Recommend C** — the column list is what the schema alone can answer and the only part both instances got wrong; which children are owned and which flag clears are domain choices with two instances, and a verb built on them is a guess, so A or B is the reopen path when a third app writes the same wrapper. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1322 — How does an app watch a list's rows: a live getter-watch in mesa, a nameable signal on `list()`, or the documented `$: { void list.rows.length; fn() }` spelling?** Found fixing `FJS-2016`. `$: list.rows, () => fn()` compiles to `watchPath(list, 'rows')` (`packages/mesa/src/runtime.js:4884`), an edge-trigger signal that only a write through the proxy fires; `list.rows` (`packages/sierra/src/resource/list.js:353`) is a getter over a private signal, so `_warnAccessorWatch` (`runtime.js:4839`) warns "never fire" and the watch is inert. The block form `$: { void list.rows.length; fn() }` compiles to a plain effect that reads the getter, so it tracks the signal and works. Red test: a component with `$: list.rows, () => { n++ }` whose list reloads — `n` stays 0 today.
  - **A** — Mesa makes a getter-only watch live: when the last segment is an accessor with no setter, `watchPath` runs the getter under an effect and fires the node's signal when what it read changes, and the warning narrows to a getter that reads nothing tracked. Fixes `list.rows`, `list.total`, `page.*`-style getters and every future reactive getter with one rule. Cost: the effect is owned by the first caller's owner and `watchPath` is also called from inside getters (`list.query`), so the node needs its own root and an eager getter evaluation per watched path — a new lifetime to get right.
  - **B** — `list()` exposes a plain signal to name (a `rows$` or `list.watch` handle), so the `$:` form watches an object path that really is written. Sierra-only, no mesa semantics change. Cost: a second spelling beside `rows` for the same data, and every reactive getter on a handle (`total`, `loading`, `hasMore`) wants one too.
  - **C** — Docs only: the sierra guide's Routes section and the mesa warning name `$: { void list.rows.length; fn() }` as the spelling for a derived or signal-backed getter. No code moves; the warning's "watch what the getter reads" advice becomes actionable. Cost: the `$: x, () => fn()` form stays inert for every signal-backed getter and the warning remains the only guard.
  - **Recommend C** — it adds no convention and moves no caller, and the warning already fires correctly for exactly the case ELA hit; A is the reopen path if a second signal-backed getter turns up beyond list handles. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1323 — Does a view absent from the schema get dropped by `migrate create`, when a hand-made view is deliberately carried through a rebuild (`FJS-D66`) and `autoMigrate` keeps one the app created?** Red test, `test/migration-history.test.ts`: history builds `view revenue` over `Order`; the schema then drops the view; `create(null, parse(WITHOUT_VIEW), 'drop_view', dir)` returns `created: false` ("the migration history already builds the schema") today, and a later `total Int → String` rebuild emits `DROP VIEW "revenue"` … `CREATE VIEW "revenue"` … `SELECT 1 FROM "revenue"`. A diff-level `droppedViews` fixes both but fails three `litestone.test.ts` tests (`a view the app created survives it too`, `a view over another table is not touched`, `a view the rebuild invalidates fails the migration`), because `diffSchemas` cannot tell a view litestone emitted from one the app wrote — views carry no name pattern the way `idx_` and `*_fts_*` do.
  - **A** — drop every view the schema does not declare, on the shadow path only: `planAgainstHistory` passes `dropUndeclaredViews`, `autoMigrate` against a live database keeps app-created views. A hand-written view inside a migration file is dropped by the next `migrate create`, and the file is the review step, so it shows as a `DROP VIEW` line.
  - **B** — mark ownership: the DDL litestone emits for a view carries a marker in its SQL text, and only a marked view is dropped, on both paths. Hand-made views survive everywhere; a view built before the marker existed is never dropped, and the `changedViews` text comparison has to ignore the marker.
  - **C** — leave behavior, and make `migrate create` print a note naming the orphan view so the author drops it by hand (what quo did). The restore on every rebuild stays.
  - **Recommend A** — the migration files are what a deploy replays, so a view there that the schema does not name is history the schema has left, and a view holds no rows to lose; B adds a mechanism and leaves every existing database unmarked.

- ~~**FJS-D1324 — Does a create or update answer a `File` column as the stored ref, or resolved like a read (`FJS-1504`)?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1324`): B — resolve the write's row like a read; `select: { f: { resolve: false } }` on the write is how a caller that wants the ref asks. `file-ref-shape` and the `file-uploads.md` fence change to read the URL, and an app needing the key reads it with `resolve: false`.** `test/file-ref-shape.test.ts` and `docs/guides/file-uploads.md` (`FJS-2422`) assert the write's row carries `avatar.mime` and `avatar.key`, the parsed ref a `File[]` item is; the row asks that `POST /api/documents` answer what every later read answers (a URL, or `null` with no `publicBase`), because the uploader is handed the storage key. Resolving in `finishWrite` (`afterRead` on a copy of `final`) turns three `file-ref-shape` tests red.
  - **A** — keep the ref on a write; document that a private store's handler must not return the row as-is. The key reaches whoever the route hands the row to.
  - **B** — resolve the write's row like a read; `select: { f: { resolve: false } }` on the write is how a caller that wants the ref asks. `file-ref-shape` and the `file-uploads.md` fence change to read the URL, and an app needing the key reads it with `resolve: false`.
  - **C** — resolve only when the store has no address to give, so a store with a `publicBase` keeps answering the ref and a private one answers `null`; two behaviors keyed on config.
  - **Recommend B** — one answer for a column on every path is what `FJS-425` and `FJS-2422` were both after, and a ref on a write is the only path where the key leaves the server unasked.

- **FJS-D1325 — Where does a static build's feed (RSS/Atom) and the Slug-history `_redirects` come from?** Found fixing `FJS-3085`. `runPostBuild` (`packages/sierra/src/postbuild/index.js:57`) writes sitemap, robots, llms and `_redirects` from the route table and has no feed step, so ghost's `feed-plugin.js` re-runs `publishedPosts()` in a `closeBundle` (step 12) outside the static-safety tap, and its `redirects-plugin.js` must be a Vite `generateBundle` plugin because step 12 runs after step 3 wrote `dist/_redirects`. The red assertion once built: a static build whose index route declares a feed writes `feed.xml` with its `count` newest items, and a `load()` behind it that reads a gated column the route does not `publishes:` fails the build by name — today neither happens, and the app's plugin reads the rows unchecked. `src/` takes no new `postbuild/` step until `test/edge.test.js` classifies it, and `FJS-D608` starts a site-shaped step as site-kit code until a consumer that is not a site-kit site needs it.
  - **A** — a `feed: [{ path, route, count, title }]` key in `sierra.config.js`, in the sitemap's position: the build serializes the named route's already-loaded rows (the ones its `load()` returned, under the same tap) as RSS 2.0, with the sitemap's XML escaping. Item fields come from the route's `.meta.js` (`title`, `date`, `url`). One new key and a step in `postbuild/`; the redirects case is not covered by it.
  - **B** — the route's `.meta.js` exports `feed: { path, count, item(row) }` and the prerender serializes it beside the page. The feed is declared where the page is, so the published set is the page's own `load()`, and a route without it costs nothing. No config key; the redirects case still needs its own answer.
  - **C** — site-kit owns it (`FJS-D608`): `siteFeed`/`siteRedirects` as Vite `generateBundle` plugins beside `config/redirects.js`, reading through `app-import.js` under a tap site-kit asks Sierra to hand it. Sierra gains nothing; every non-site-kit static app (ghost's `site/`) still writes its own.
  - **D** — state it and change nothing: a feed is an app plugin, and `sierra/CLAUDE.md` says to emit it at `generateBundle` (not `closeBundle`) and to wrap its reads in `createReadRecorder`'s tap. Smallest; leaves the feed outside the gate check unless the app remembers.
  - **Recommend B** — the feed and the page read the same rows in the same `load()`, so the published set is decided once and the gate check covers it for free (§ V.5); A restates the route by name in a second file and drifts. Confidence 2: whether `_redirects` from a Slug history is the same shape (rows, not a page) or needs a separate seam is unpriced.

- **FJS-D1326 — Should `/fjs-sqlite/sqlite3.wasm` get a long Cache-Control when its name is unhashed and the loader beside it revalidates?** The module fetches its `.wasm` by its own published name (`local-db-plugin.js`), so the file cannot be content-addressed, and `cacheFor` sends `max-age=0, must-revalidate` for anything outside `assets/`. `immutable` there would pin a wasm to a loader that revalidates, so a sqlite-wasm upgrade could pair a new `index.js` with a year-old binary.
  - **A** — leave the revalidate default: the service worker precaches `/fjs-sqlite/*` (`test/offline-shell.test.js`) and a 304 keeps Chrome's HTTP-cache entry, which is what its compiled-module cache hangs off.
  - **B** — put a content hash in the directory name (`fjs-sqlite-<hash>/`) and serve it `immutable`; `WASM_URL` in `virtual-sierra.js` then needs the hash at build.
  - **C** — a bounded `max-age` (a day) on `/fjs-sqlite/*` in `_headers`, accepting a stale pairing for that long after an upgrade.
  - **Recommend A** — measure Chrome's compile cache on a revalidated load before paying for B; C trades correctness for a number nobody has measured.

- **FJS-D1327 — When a wildcard cron's minutes pass with the process down, does the scheduler report them, or stay silent as `FJS-D144` leaves it?** Found fixing `FJS-3108`. `_tickWildcard` (`packages/caravan/src/cron.ts`) asks only whether the clock says this minute, so a restart fires the current minute and `jobs.db` keeps `cron:probe:29869534` and `:29869536` with nothing for `:29869535`; the gap is a fact only a reader diffing the minutes can see. A replay is out (`FJS-D144`: a past minute cannot be probed), so the question is whether the gap is reported at all. Red test, `test/next-fire.test.ts` shape with an injected `now`: register `* * * * *`, tick at minute 10, move `now` to minute 13, tick — assert a gap of 11 and 12 is reported once; today nothing is.
  - **A** — an Observer on the job, `onMissed(ctx: { name, from, to, minutes })`, fired by the scheduler when a wildcard tick finds the last minute it fired (read from `jobs.db`'s newest `cron:<name>:` id, so it survives the restart) more than one behind now. The app records a "no check" row itself. Adds one option and a jobs.db read at start; a throw is swallowed like any observer.
  - **B** — a row: the scheduler dispatches each missed minute under `cron:<name>:<minute>` with status `missed` and no handler run, so the id stays the idempotency key and a reader diffing `scheduledFor` finds a row. Adds a terminal status to the jobs table that every list, count and cleanup must learn.
  - **C** — no code: state in `caravan/CLAUDE.md` that a wildcard gap is a fact the app reads off `scheduledFor`, and ship nothing. A probe app diffs the minutes itself.
  - **Recommend A** — the fact is the app's to map (a 'no check' Check is statuspage's noun, not Caravan's), and B puts an app meaning into the queue's status column; C leaves every probing app writing the same jobs.db diff.

- **FJS-D1328 — How does a register row get an edit that is not an append: a replace on `amend`, a verb per edit, or does the append-only rule stand?** Found fixing `FJS-2751`. `amendUnguarded` (`packages/cli/core/amend.js`) only ever writes `${kept.text} · ${added}`, and its header states the rule: what a row already says is how the defect was measured, so an amend that can drop it loses the evidence. The only removal is `drop`, one whole ` · ` segment, reached by `register:repro` and not by a flag. The audit's ~640 hand writes are three kinds of edit the writer cannot do: retarget a link whose file moved, renumber an `FJS-D` id, reword a cell. Red test, `test/amend.test.js` shape: a row whose Detail holds a link to a moved file; `amendIssue({ root, id, replace: ['old', 'new'] })` — today the option is ignored and the cell is refused for having no `detail`.
  - **A** — `--replace "old=>new"` on `amend`, Detail cell of an open row, `old` must occur exactly once or the write is refused; `--detail` stays the append and the two may be given together. Adds one option and relaxes the header's rule to "a replacement names what it replaces". Covers a reword and one link; a renumber across many rows is still a loop of calls, and a § Closed row stays refused.
  - **B** — a verb per edit, none touching `amend`: `fli register:retarget <old> <new>` (every register link resolving to `old`, the pass `archive.js` already runs when it moves rows), and `fli register:renumber <old-id> <new-id>` (the id in its row and every citation, with `register:check` as the put-back). A reword has no verb and stays hand-written. Adds two commands and two nouns, each owning the whole-register edit rather than one row.
  - **C** — no code: a hand edit to a cell is an `Edit` call on one line, which `register:check` already grades afterward; the 339 python replaces were the habit of a model without a writer, not a missing capability. State it in `cli/CLAUDE.md` and ship nothing.
  - **Recommend A** — all three audited edits are a text swap inside one row, one flag covers them with the put-back `amend` already has, and B's whole-register verbs are worth building only after a swap proves too small, which `FJS-2392` and `FJS-2328` would show first.

- ~~**FJS-D1331 — Which spelling does `ctx` keep for the raw request and the scoped client, and where do `_cleanups`/`_afterCommit`/`_outbox` go?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1331`): A — `ctx.$raw` → `ctx.raw` (Hono `c.req.raw`, Fastify `request.raw`); `$.db` is the one spelling of the scoped client and `ctx.locals.db` is deleted; the three `_*` fields move behind a module-private `Symbol`. `VOCABULARY.md` § The sign `$` loses `ctx.$raw` in the same change.** Found fixing `FJS-2956`. Measured 2026-10-19: `ctx.$raw` is the one `$`-field on the public interface (14 files in `packages/`, 2 in `../fjs-prototypes`); the scoped client is `$.db` (63 files here, 147 in the prototypes) and `ctx.locals.db` (53 here, 4 there); `_cleanups`/`_afterCommit`/`_outbox` sit on `ServiceContext` with no outside reader. `VOCABULARY.md` § The sign `$` names `ctx.$raw` and moves with any answer.
  - **A** — `ctx.$raw` → `ctx.raw` (Hono `c.req.raw`, Fastify `request.raw`); `$.db` is the one spelling of the scoped client and `ctx.locals.db` is deleted; the three `_*` fields move behind a module-private `Symbol`. `VOCABULARY.md` § The sign `$` loses `ctx.$raw` in the same change.
  - **B** — A, but `ctx.locals.db` wins and `$.db` goes. Moves the 147 prototype files that already write `$.db`.
  - **C** — hide the `_*` fields only; `$raw` and both client spellings stay.
  - **Recommend A** — `$.db` is what outside authors already write, and `raw` takes the one stray `$` out of the field names Invariant 10 keeps for transport syntax. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1332 — What shape does an auth callback take, and does `onRegister` survive beside `onRegistered`?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1332`): A — every callback takes one event object (`onPasswordResetRequested({ email, token })`), and `onRegister` is deleted: `onRegistered` already refuses by throwing. `canStartSupport` keeps its name, a Guard by `FJS-D06`, and takes one object too.** Found fixing `FJS-2961`. `onPasswordResetRequested(email, token)`, `onEmailVerificationRequested(email, token)` and `canStartSupport(operator, subjectId)` are positional where `onCredentialChanged({ … })` takes one object; `onRegister` (6 files, 0 prototype) and `onRegistered` (9, 4) both exist, and `FJS-D520` makes `onRegistered` the in-transaction hook whose throw refuses.
  - **A** — every callback takes one event object (`onPasswordResetRequested({ email, token })`), and `onRegister` is deleted: `onRegistered` already refuses by throwing. `canStartSupport` keeps its name, a Guard by `FJS-D06`, and takes one object too.
  - **B** — one object each, and `onRegister` becomes `canRegister`, a Guard.
  - **C** — one object each, and the hook tier is spelled `before*`/`after*` (Better Auth's `databaseHooks`), superseding `FJS-D520`'s `onRegistered`.
  - **Recommend A** — one concept fewer with no prototype caller, and `FJS-D520` stands; one object per callback is Auth.js's `signIn({ user, account })` and Better Auth's `sendResetPassword({ user, url, token })`. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1333 — Where do junction's bare-verb exports live?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1333`): A — subpaths: `@frontierjs/junction/envelope` (`single`, `list`), a new `/hooks` (every built-in hook), a new `/bearer` (`header`, `cookie`, `authorization`, `bearerClaim`, `BEARER`, `bearerOf`). Call sites are unchanged; imports move.** Found fixing `FJS-3008`. `.` exports `single`/`list` (envelope, 0 outside importers), the built-in hooks `protect`/`allow`/`paginate` and kin (0), and the bearer sources `header`/`cookie`/`authorization` (1 example file, 19 prototype files). `FJS-2979` moved litestone's bare verbs to a subpath; `FJS-D1077` keeps bare NOUNS (plugins) in `.`.
  - **A** — subpaths: `@frontierjs/junction/envelope` (`single`, `list`), a new `/hooks` (every built-in hook), a new `/bearer` (`header`, `cookie`, `authorization`, `bearerClaim`, `BEARER`, `bearerOf`). Call sites are unchanged; imports move.
  - **B** — rename in place: `singleResult`/`listResult`, `fromHeader`/`fromCookie`/`fromAuthorization`, `protectFields`/`allowFields`/`paginateHook`.
  - **C** — only `single`/`list` leave `.`.
  - **Recommend A** — `FJS-2979` is the in-domain sibling, and the import path says what `header` means while `from: header('x')` keeps reading. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1334 — How does the client schema tell a form that a string column is a secret?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1334`): A — `format: 'password'`, OpenAPI's own hint ("a hint to UIs to obscure input"), on an `@encrypted`, `@secret` or `@hashed` string; `controlFor` answers a password input for it. `@hashed` keeps `writeOnly`.** Found fixing `FJS-2259`. `generateJsonSchema` emits only `x-sortable`/`x-filterable: 'encrypted'` for `@encrypted`, so `controlFor` answers a text input; `@hashed` emits `writeOnly`, which `controlFor` does not read either.
  - **A** — `format: 'password'`, OpenAPI's own hint ("a hint to UIs to obscure input"), on an `@encrypted`, `@secret` or `@hashed` string; `controlFor` answers a password input for it. `@hashed` keeps `writeOnly`.
  - **B** — a new `x-secret: true`, read the same way; leaves `format` free for `@email` and kin.
  - **C** — reuse `writeOnly`; wrong for `@encrypted`, which an authorized caller reads back.
  - **Recommend A** — an existing standard every OpenAPI consumer already reads. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1335 — How does `migrate create` see a `@scale` change, which leaves the DDL unchanged?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-19 (`FJS-D1335`): A — the scale is in the DDL: the column's CHECK constraint is named with it (`units_scale_6`), the replayed history carries it, and the differ sees the change. A table with rows is refused with the `UPDATE` to write by hand; an empty one rebuilds.** Found fixing `FJS-2140`. The CHECK is the same ±2^53 at every scale, and `FJS-D123` diffs the replayed history, so `@scale(9)` → `@scale(6)` answers "schema is in sync" and a rebuild copies the integers verbatim, every value now 1000x.
  - **A** — the scale is in the DDL: the column's CHECK constraint is named with it (`units_scale_6`), the replayed history carries it, and the differ sees the change. A table with rows is refused with the `UPDATE` to write by hand; an empty one rebuilds.
  - **B** — A, and a widening (lossless) emits the multiply itself; only a narrowing is refused.
  - **C** — a `_litestone_columns` table the migrator writes and compares.
  - **Recommend A** — the DDL stays the one record the differ reads, and a refusal that names the statement is the smallest correct answer. Confidence 2.

- ~~**FJS-D1336 — How does a basecamp write name a User the caller cannot read (User is self-only) — `workspaces.addMember`'s invitee and a bot's API key?**~~ **Answered 2026-10-20 (`FJS-D1336`): A — `addMember` stays on the caller's client and names `userId` under `system:` after the service has found the user, with `WorkspaceMember.userId` made `@system`; a bot key stays on the caller's client and a `User` row policy lets a caller who may issue one (level 5, `ApiKey`'s own `@@gate`) read the bots that are members of their workspace — no human row widens, and a viewer still reads one row.** Found when today's cleanup moved both writes to `asSystem()`, which drops the capability guard and the caller as the trail's actor. `FJS-D576` makes a foreign key a read of its parent; `FJS-1953` lets a call's `system:` list lift an `@system` key the app names. A bot key's `userId` comes from the form, and sierra strips an `@system` column before sending.
  - **A** — `addMember` stays on the caller's client and names `userId` under `system:` after the service has found the user, with `WorkspaceMember.userId` made `@system`; a bot key stays on the caller's client and a `User` row policy lets a caller who may issue one (level 5, `ApiKey`'s own `@@gate`) read the bots that are members of their workspace — no human row widens, and a viewer still reads one row.
  - **B** — both writes stay `asSystem()` after the service's checks.
  - **C** — every workspace member reads the workspace's bots.
  - **Recommend A** — the guard and the actor stay the caller's, and the one widening is declared in the schema (Invariant 6). Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1341 — How does a multiselect over a `Model.verb` enum list show its members grouped by model: MultiSelect takes Select's grouped options shape, a `groupBy` prop, a capability-matrix control, or the flat list stays?** `FJS-3151`: basecamp's `capabilities Capability[]` (`packages/basecamp/db/schema.lite:907`) reaches `controlFor` as `{ control: 'multiselect', task: 'select', allowNew: false }` (`packages/sierra/src/resource/field-rules.js:776`), and the 78 members (`'Environment.create' | 'Server.reboot' | …`, `packages/basecamp/db/schema.d.ts:115`) render as one unbroken list. `Select.mesa` already takes `{ group, options }[]` and draws an `<optgroup>` per group (`packages/ui/components/forms/Select.mesa:14`, `:105`); `MultiSelect.mesa` takes only a flat list (`packages/ui/components/forms/MultiSelect.mesa:28`).
  - **A** — MultiSelect takes Select's grouped shape, `{ group, options }[]`, and `controlFor` emits it for an enum list whose every member is `Model.verb`, splitting on the dot. No new prop: the grouping is derived from the members, and the options shape is one across both controls.
  - **B** — a `groupBy` prop on MultiSelect (a separator or a function), and `controlFor` passes `groupBy: '.'`. Cost: a second way to say grouping beside Select's shape, and a prop every hand-written caller must know.
  - **C** — a capability-matrix control: models as rows, verbs as columns, a checkbox per member that exists, chosen by `controlFor` for a `Model.verb` list. Reads best at 78 members, but coins a control and a `task` with one caller.
  - **D** — the flat list stays, and an app that wants groups passes its own options. Cost: every `@@capabilities` app writes the same split, and the generated form stays unbroken.
  - **Recommend A** — Select is the sibling that already decided this shape, so A adds no convention and the grouping is derived rather than restated. C is the reopen path if a grouped dropdown still scans badly at basecamp's size. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1342 — Does a client subscription under a service's filename spelling hear the canonical name's events: the client folds the name by `FJS-D159`'s rule, a subscription under an alias is refused by name, or it stays deaf and documented?** `FJS-3124`: `deriveName` folds `log-chunks` to `logChunks` and mounts the stem as an alias (`packages/junction/src/core/loader.ts:219`), so a CALL under either spelling reaches one service and announces as `logChunks created`. The client keys a frame by the name it was handed — `this._services.get(serviceName)?._receive(method, data)` (`packages/junction/src/client/index.ts:2507`) — so `service('log-chunks').on('created')` received 0 of 56 frames while `service('logChunks')` received all 56. The client holds no manifest, so it cannot ask the server which name is canonical.
  - **A** — the client folds: `client.service(name)` keys its proxy by `camel(name)` from `@frontierjs/toolbelt/inflect` (which already answers `log-chunks` → `logChunks`, `product_variants` → `productVariants`), and `deriveName` uses the same `camel` so the rule has one owner. `service('log-chunks')` and `service('logChunks')` are then one proxy, and the alias covers subscriptions as D159 says it covers calls. Cost: a service an app registers by hand under a name with a dash is reachable for calls but heard under the folded name only.
  - **B** — the client refuses: `service(name)` throws on a name that `camel()` changes, naming the canonical spelling. Nothing is deaf, but every call that works today under the filename spelling stops, which takes back D159's alias on the client.
  - **C** — it stays as it is, and the client's docs say to subscribe under the canonical name. The deaf listener stays silent, which is the defect.
  - **Recommend A** — D159 already ruled one canonical spelling derived by a rule, and A applies that rule where the name enters the client instead of a second spelling living beside it. B moves every caller; C leaves a wrong answer nothing reports. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1343 — Do the five blessed words with few prose hits — Vector, Fault, Gesture, Pivot, Adversary — stay blessed, or are the ones with no identifier in the tree demoted to open?** `FJS-2456` counted capitalized prose hits outside the doctrine files: Vector 3, Fault 1, Gesture 8, Pivot 5, Adversary 5. A lowercase and table sweep finds four in use: Vector as `served-path-vectors.json` and `audit-directives-vectors.test.ts` in junction's suite, Pivot in `packages/cli/core/deploy-plan.js:238` and `packages/cli/db/deploy.lite`, Adversary as the row key of `THREATS.md:9`, Gesture in `ARCHITECT.md:75` with `IDEAS/mesa-ir.md`'s open question *Is a handler's event name a DOM event or a Gesture?* waiting on it. Fault is ruled (`FJS-D655`) and its definition cites `FAULT_KINDS` in `@frontierjs/toolbelt/fault`, which does not exist yet: `IDEAS/failure-handling.md:36` records it as absent and owes the build. The row's other half, `fli ws:terms` misreporting Run, Method and Migration as `definedUnseen`, is a defect and stays the row's.
  - **A** — all five stay blessed. Each is a ruled word (`FJS-D385`, `FJS-D636`, `FJS-D655`, `FJS-D656`), four are in use under a spelling the case-sensitive count did not see, and Fault's code is owed by a paper. `FJS-2456` narrows to the `ws:terms` misreport.
  - **B** — Fault and Vector go to `open` until an identifier carries them: Fault's definition names a constant nobody can import, and Vector lives only in test fixture names. Cost: reopens two rulings, and Fault comes back the day `toolbelt/fault` lands.
  - **C** — a threshold: any blessed word under ten prose hits with no source identifier goes to `open`, graded by `fli ws:terms`. Cost: a rule that demotes a word for being precise and rarely needed, which Adversary and Pivot are by design.
  - **Recommend A** — the low counts measure capitalization, not use, and every word has a ruling behind it, so A moves nothing and reopens nothing. B is the reopen path for Fault if `IDEAS/failure-handling.md` is withdrawn rather than built. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1344 — Does example's payroll take a measured hour: a third basis whose lines are the gross, a Payslip drafted from lines rather than a window, or does example stay a contract payroll and the workforce shape stay the prototype's?** `FJS-1246`: `PayWindowRow.basis` is `'salary' | 'hourly'` (`example/api/src/domain/payroll/employment.ts:93`), `annualGross` is `rate * hoursPerWeek * 52` (`employment.ts:312`), and `draftPayslip` takes one window and emits one `basicPay` line (`example/api/src/domain/payroll/payslip.ts:98`, `:116`). A connectteam timesheet pushed through it came out €12.00 wrong per fortnight from rounding 3.5 h/week to an `Int`, lost its per-line `rateSnapshot`, and had nowhere to put overtime. The row says itself that nothing here is a defect in what `example` ships.
  - **A** — a third basis, `measured`: `PayWindowRow` carries `lines: { onDate, kind, minutes, rate }[]`, `annualGross` is not called for it, and `draftPayslip` emits one earning line per `kind`, so overtime gets a line outside `arrears.ts`. Cost: every reader of `basis` grows a branch.
  - **B** — `draftPayslip` gains a second entry that takes lines and no window, and `PayWindow` stays the contract path. Cost: two ways in to one Payslip, and the window's period arithmetic restated for lines.
  - **C** — example stays a contract payroll. A workforce product's measured pay is the prototype's own domain (`fjs-prototypes/connectteam`), and `FJS-1246` closes as out of scope for example, the measurement recorded in the connectteam PLAN where it was taken.
  - **Recommend C** — no framework package is touched by any option, so this is app modelling, and C moves no caller and adds no shape to the kitchen sink for a product it is not. B is the reopen path when a second stressor needs measured pay through example's payroll. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1346 — Are the two orphaned npm names, `@frontierjs/utils` and `@frontierjs/datetime-kit`, deprecated with a pointer to toolbelt, also unpublished, or left as they are?** `FJS-274`: both folded into `@frontierjs/toolbelt` on 2026-08-15 and their source is gone, so `@frontierjs/utils@0.1.0` and `@frontierjs/datetime-kit@0.0.10` stay installable and point nowhere; `IDEAS/datetime-kit.md` found the latter's `extend()` and `add(d, '1 day')` throwing on every call. npm's unpublish policy (https://docs.npmjs.com/policies/unpublish) allows removing a package older than 72 hours only when nothing on the registry depends on it, it had under 300 downloads in the last week, and it has a single owner; a removed `name@version` can never be published again. `npm deprecate` (https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/commands/npm-deprecate) leaves the tarball and prints the message at every install, and is undone with an empty message.
  - **A** — `npm deprecate` both, the message naming `@frontierjs/toolbelt` and the subpath (`/datetime` for datetime-kit). Reversible, and every install shows the pointer the row asks for.
  - **B** — A, then unpublish both where npm's policy allows it, so the broken datetime-kit cannot be installed at all. Irreversible, and refused outright if either has a dependent.
  - **C** — leave both as they are. A reader finds a FrontierJS name that throws, with no pointer.
  - **Recommend A** — it is what npm offers for exactly this case, it is undoable, and it gives the pointer the row wants; B adds only the removal of a version nobody is told to install, at an irreversible cost. Running it is an outward-facing action, so the ruling also authorizes it. Confidence 3.

- **FJS-D1347 — Where do fli's project commands live, now that `cli/src/routes/` is also the app CLI surface's hand-written routes?** `FJS-1352`: `routesDir` defaults to `cli/src/routes` (`packages/cli/core/config.js:18`, and `fli new` writes it into `.fli.json`, `packages/cli/commands/project/new.md:357`), while `FJS-D397` made `cli/` a surface whose `src/routes/` holds the app's own commands (`routeFiles`, `packages/mcp/src/client/routes.ts:83`). Each program reads the other's files as noise only because one takes `.md` and the other `.js`/`.mjs`/`.ts`. The fli files that would move today: `cli/src/routes/shortcut/` at this root (4), `example/cli/src/routes/shortcut/go-time.md` and `packages/cli/cli/src/routes/hello/`. Basecamp's `cli/src/routes/servers/status.js` is the surface's.
  - **A** — fli's default moves to `.fli/commands/` at the project root, beside `.fli.json` and in the same shape as the `~/.fli/` fli already keeps per user (`packages/cli/core/registry.js:33`). The existing project commands move in the same change, and `fli new` and `fli make:command` write the new path. The `cli/` surface keeps `src/routes/`.
  - **B** — the surface moves instead, to `cli/src/commands/`, and fli keeps `cli/src/routes/`. Cost: the surface loses the rule its own header states, *the path is the name, the way a file under `web/src/routes/` is a page*, and the surface is the newer, ruled one.
  - **C** — one directory with two readers split by extension, written down. One path still spells two commands in two programs, each seeing half.
  - **Recommend A** — fli's project commands are tooling, not a surface, and a dot directory at the root is where tooling lives (`.fli.json`, `~/.fli/`, `.claude/commands/`). It also takes a fli-only directory out of Invariant 3's surface layout. Confidence 2.

- **FJS-D1348 — Does Mesa accept a CDATA section inside an `<svg>` or `<math>` subtree?** `FJS-966`: the compiler recognizes `<![CDATA[` only to refuse it by name and say what to write (`packages/mesa/src/compiler.js:1991`). The HTML parsing spec accepts a CDATA section in foreign content only, where it becomes a text node, and Vue's compiler follows it: `@vue/compiler-core` has a parse error `CDATA_IN_HTML_CONTENT` for one outside foreign content. Svelte has no handling at all, per the row.
  - **A** — accept it in a foreign subtree as LITERAL text: no `{` interpolation, the content escaped into one text node. Outside foreign content it is refused as today. This is a literal-text escape with a rule of its own, and so a language feature.
  - **B** — accept it in a foreign subtree and interpolate `{` as in ordinary text. Smallest parser change, but the wrapper's whole meaning is *literal*, so a pasted `{` inside one becomes an expression, which is the surprise A avoids.
